# Master ExecPlan — Email Operations Workspace

> **Run ID:** `2026-08-10-eow-master-execplan`
> **Mode:** GRAPH containing bounded LOOPs (`.agents/orchestration-policy.yaml` → `default_after_approval`)
> **State:** `.agents/runs/2026-08-10-eow-master-execplan/state.json`
> **Status:** `waiting` — planning complete, held at **PLAN APPROVAL GATE**
>
> This is a living document. It is written so that an agent with **no conversation
> history** can read this file plus `state.json` and resume exactly at the current
> checkpoint. Update the **Progress**, **Surprises & Discoveries**, **Decision Log**
> and **Acceptance Evidence** sections after every node transition.

---

## 1. Purpose / Big Picture

Build the Email Operations Workspace from a reviewed specification plus a runnable
skeleton into a production-ready modular monolith that a tenant's operations team uses to:

manage recipients, lists, tags and custom data → author versioned HTML email templates
with merge variables → compose a campaign against a resolved audience → freeze an
immutable snapshot → send now or on a schedule → watch live progress → recover from
partial failures → and review durable history and notifications.

**What "done" means for the whole project** is defined by `docs/delivery/definition-of-done.md`
and expanded in §21. The short version: rule → contract → code → test → observability →
deployment evidence is closed for all 118 business rules and 16 notification rules, all
159 catalogued test cases execute, the approved UI is reproduced at three viewports, and
`docker compose --env-file .env up -d --build --wait` still brings up the whole stack.

**Where the project actually is today** (verified, §4): the specification is complete and
high quality; the code is a skeleton with essentially no domain behaviour, no persistence
layer, no authentication and three unit tests. The gap between catalogue and code is the
entire project.

### Commands that work on this host

```bash
corepack enable --install-directory "$LOCALAPPDATA/corepack-shims"
```

Then prepend that directory to `PATH` for the session. Without it, `pnpm check` fails
because the `check` script shells out to `pnpm typecheck` (see BLK-01).

| Purpose | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Install | `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` |
| Full baseline | `pnpm check` (= typecheck + test + build) |
| UI handoff status | `python scripts/ui_handoff.py status` |
| UI handoff register | `python scripts/ui_handoff.py register` |
| Local infra | `pnpm infra:up` / `pnpm infra:down` |
| Compose validation | `docker compose --env-file .env config --quiet` |
| One-command deploy | `docker compose --env-file .env up -d --build --wait` |
| Deploy smoke | `python scripts/smoke_deploy.py` |

## 3. Sources of Truth and Precedence

Applied in this order when two documents disagree:

1. **Behaviour** → `catalog/ba-rules.json` + `catalog/notification-rules.json`
2. **Presentation and interaction** → `design-reference/ui-handoff-v2/source/`
3. **HTTP** → `contracts/openapi.yaml`
4. **Realtime** → `contracts/asyncapi.yaml`
5. **Data baseline** → `database/migrations/*.sql`
6. **Architecture** → Accepted ADRs in `docs/adr/`

Everything else — `catalog/entities.json`, `catalog/api-baseline.json`,
`catalog/open-decisions.json`, `catalog/business-gaps.json`, the Word/Excel references —
is **reviewed intent**: authoritative about what was analysed, not about what is contracted.
Where intent and contract disagree, the contract wins and the difference is recorded in
the Decision Log.

**Conflict rule** (AGENTS.md §2.1): business rules control behaviour, the UI handoff controls
presentation. Only a conflict that cannot be resolved without changing business meaning
*or* redesigning an approved screen becomes a STOP gate. None was found during planning.

## 4. Current-State Assessment

Full matrix: `implementation-inventory.yaml`. Verified with the commands in §4.1, not inferred.

### 4.1 Baseline evidence

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `python scripts/ui_handoff.py status` | **exit 0** — `{"status":"ready","registered":false,"file_count":29763,"total_bytes":720335586,"sha256":"4cd6c1a8fae26d1caf94a0f9d617711443494022cdee51a08cab3c21aa3adbf3"}` |
| `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` | **exit 0** — 213 packages, lockfile passed supply-chain policy (267 entries) |
| `pnpm check` | **exit 0** — typecheck 5/5; tests: 4 apps "No test files found", `runtime-orchestration` 3 pass; build 5/5, `apps/web` dist 369.36 kB |
| `docker compose --env-file .env config --quiet` | **not run** — `.env` absent; creating it is outside the planning scope |
| `docker compose --env-file .env.deploy.example config --quiet` | **exit 1** — `required variable EOW_POSTGRES_PASSWORD is missing a value`. This *is* the DEPLOY-001 expected behaviour, so it counts as passing evidence for that case. |
| `docker compose config --quiet` (dummy secrets in env) | **exit 0** — `compose.yaml` interpolates and validates |
| Toolchain | node v22.20.0, pnpm 11.21.0 (corepack), python 3.12.10, docker 29.4.2 |

**The baseline is green, and that is misleading.** `pnpm check` passes with zero application
tests because all four apps run `vitest run --passWithNoTests`. Every milestone gate must
therefore assert a *test count*, not an exit code.

### 4.2 Summary by classification

| Classification | Areas |
| --- | --- |
| **implemented** | *(none)* |
| **starter** | configuration (`ConfigModule` only), worker (8 stub handlers), scheduler (real tick, stub jobs), realtime gateway (connect event only), scheduling (in-memory `Map`), one-command deployment (valid, never booted) |
| **mock** | authentication, preview, campaign draft, sending, realtime progress, notification centre, history |
| **contract_only** | tenant, audit log, error model, recipients, lists, tags, custom fields, template versions, variable extraction, immutable snapshot, backup/restore |
| **absent** | RBAC, import/bulk update, audience query, provider adapter, webhook reconciliation, quota, observability, security |

### 4.3 The five findings that shape the plan

1. **`apps/api` has no database layer.** No TypeORM, no `pg`, no entity, no use of
   `DATABASE_URL`. Every persistence-dependent slice is blocked until a foundation node
   lands it. This is the single biggest sequencing constraint. *(BLK-04)*
2. **`contracts/openapi.yaml` covers 13 paths with almost no schemas**, while
   `catalog/api-baseline.json` describes 30 endpoint groups. The HTTP source of truth is
   a stub that must grow additively before downstream nodes can generate clients. *(BLK-05, DRIFT-02)*
3. **Six aggregates named in `catalog/entities.json` do not exist in the schema**:
   `sender_accounts`, `campaign_audience_rules`, `campaign_executions`, `message_attempts`,
   `delivery_events`, `async_jobs`/`job_items`, `idempotency_keys`. Forward migrations only —
   `001_initial.sql` is published and checksum-guarded. *(DRIFT-03)*
4. **Confirmed contract drift in shipped code**: `apps/web/src/App.tsx` subscribes to
   `campaign.progress.updated`; the contract event is `campaign.progress`. Today's realtime
   demo cannot work. *(DRIFT-01)*
5. **The approved UI is far richer than `apps/web`.** 13 screens and 39 overlays in the
   handoff versus one demo card in `apps/web`. The handoff also carries ~20 actions that
   resolve with a toast and no state change. Full inventory: `ui-inventory.yaml`.

### 4.4 Test reality

159 catalogued cases. 3 automated tests, all in `packages/runtime-orchestration`.
**Zero** business rules have an automated test. No integration, contract, E2E, security,
recovery, accessibility or visual layer exists.

## 5. Scope and Non-Goals

### In scope

All seven milestones from `docs/00-start-here.md`, delivered as vertical slices:
foundation and identity; recipient data; templates; campaigns; execution; experience;
hardening. Including: forward migrations, OpenAPI/AsyncAPI expansion, backend modules,
incremental UI migration into `apps/web`, RBAC and tenant isolation, audit, observability,
the full test pyramid, one-command deployment validation, backup/restore rehearsal and
operator documentation.

### Non-goals

- **Production deployment.** ADR-024 and `.agents/approval-policy.yaml` keep it a STOP gate.
- **Redesigning approved screens.** Adding the six contractually required states is not a redesign.
- **Choosing a commercial email provider.** OD-05 is open; MVP ships the SMTP adapter behind
  the ADR-014 interface, targeting Mailpit.
- **High-availability topology.** Compose single-host is the ADR-024 baseline.
- **Editing published migrations or Accepted ADRs.** Forward migrations and superseding ADRs only.
- **Real secrets or paid external services.** Locally generated development secrets only.
- **Multi-locale UI.** `BR-NOT-015` requires storing message *keys and parameters*; rendering
  additional locales is not in MVP.

## 6. Assumptions

Recorded because they were resolved by judgement rather than instruction. Each has a
Decision Log entry. None blocks planning.

| # | Assumption | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A-01 | The single-page handoff becomes addressable routes in `apps/web` | `BR-NOT-010` deep links, `BR-HIS-003` drill-down, `BR-AUTH-004` route permissions cannot work without them. Presentation is unchanged. |
| A-02 | `globals.css` transfers verbatim | It is keyed on semantic class names (`.app-shell`, `.compose-grid`, …), so copying the DOM structure preserves visuals by construction. |
| A-03 | Geist / Geist Mono are self-hosted in `apps/web` | The handoff loads them via `next/font/google`; `apps/web` has no Next.js. Same CSS variable names, so `globals.css` is untouched. |
| A-04 | Raw SQL stays the migration mechanism | ADR-024 makes `database/migrate.sh` the deployment contract; it is newer than ADR-007. TypeORM is used for entity mapping only. |
| A-05 | Socket.IO, not SSE | ADR-009 Accepted + `contracts/asyncapi.yaml` outrank OD-08 and `api-baseline.json`'s `GET /campaigns/{id}/events`. |
| A-06 | `tenant` remains the table name | It is the published data baseline; `catalog/entities.json`'s `organizations` is reviewed intent. |
| A-07 | MVP provider is SMTP against Mailpit | OD-05 is open; ADR-014's adapter boundary makes the choice reversible. |
| A-08 | Snapshot freezes at schedule confirmation | OD-03 recommendation, confirmed by ADR-013 (Accepted). |
| A-09 | `BR-NOT-*` need newly authored tests | `catalog/test-cases.json` has no `TC-NOT-*` prefix. New IDs are recorded as `authored-in-run`. |
| A-10 | The 768×1024 tablet render uses the desktop grid | The handoff breakpoints are 1100px and 760px, so 768px falls between them. This is captured as the baseline, not corrected. |

## 7. Agent Graph

Per `.agents/agent-graph.yaml`: no implicit dependency, no cycle, no downstream start
before required outputs, every code node has verification, the completion gate re-checks
Definition of Done, and state is persisted after every transition.

Each implementation and verification node runs a bounded LOOP
(`observe → hypothesize → act → verify → reflect → checkpoint`) with the
`.agents/agent-loop.yaml` budget: **5 iterations per node, 3 per identical failure class,
new evidence required each iteration**.

### 7.1 Graph shape

```
intake ──> discovery ──┬─> ui_intake ──> ui_intake_register ──┐
                       └─> plan ──> arch_contracts ──> traceability_init ──┤
                                                                            v
   M1-S1 ──┬─> M1-S2 ──┬─> M1-GATE ──> M2-S1 ──┬─> M2-S2 ──┬─> M2-GATE ──> M3-S1 ──> M3-S2 ──┬─> M3-GATE
           └─> M1-S3 ──┘                        ├─> M2-S3 ──┤                        └─> M3-S3 ──┘
                                                └─> M2-S4 ──┘
   M3-GATE ──> M4-S1 ──> M4-S2 ──> M4-S3 ──> M4-S4 ──> M4-GATE
   M4-GATE ──> M5-S2 ──> M5-S3 ──> M5-S4 ──> M5-GATE        (M5-S1 may start after M1-GATE)
   M5-GATE ──> M6-S1 ──┬─> M6-S3 ──> M6-GATE                (M6-S2 may start after M1-GATE)
                       └─> M6-S2 ──┘
   M6-GATE ──┬─> M7-S1 ──┐
             ├─> M7-S2 ──┤
             ├─> M7-S3 ──┼─> M7-S5 ──> M7-GATE ──> packaging ──> qa_security_final ──┐
             └─> M7-S4 ──┘                    │                                       ├─> completion_gate
                                              └──────────> documentation_final ───────┘
                                                                  ^
                                              traceability_init ──┘
```

M7-GATE joins all five M7 slices directly (not only `M7-S5`), so the join condition is
explicit rather than transitive. `documentation_final` also depends on `traceability_init`,
because the registry it seeds is what the final documentation node verifies.

### 7.2 Foundation nodes

| Node | Purpose · Depends on · Outputs · Success · Retry |
| --- | --- |
| **`intake`** | Record approval scope and exclusions. — deps: none. — out: `state.json.approval`. — success: scope and exclusions written. — retry 1. — **completed** |
| **`discovery`** | Verified current state, baseline, blockers, contract drift. — deps: `intake`. — out: `implementation-inventory.yaml`. — success: all 30 areas classified with evidence; baseline commands run. — retry 2. — **completed** |
| **`ui_intake`** | Inventory the approved UI. — deps: `discovery`. — out: `ui-inventory.yaml`. — success: status `ready`; routes, screens, components, overlays, states, assets, tokens, mocks and dependencies enumerated. — retry 2. — **completed** |
| **`ui_intake_register`** | Pin the handoff fingerprint. — deps: `ui_intake`. — cmd: `python scripts/ui_handoff.py register`. — out: `installed.json`. — success: `status` reports `registered: true`. — retry 2. — **pending, blocks all frontend nodes** |
| **`plan`** | This document. — deps: `discovery`. — out: `EXECPLAN.md`, `traceability-plan.yaml`, `state.json`. — success: all 7 milestones covered, every code node has verification, plan is resumable. — retry 2. — **completed** |
| **`arch_contracts`** | Stabilise the foundations everything else needs. — deps: `plan` **+ human plan approval**. — see §7.3. |
| **`traceability_init`** | Create `traceability.csv` (134 rule rows) and `screen-catalog.yaml` (13 screens, 39 overlays). — deps: `arch_contracts`, `ui_intake_register`. — success: every rule and screen present with status `not_started`. — retry 2. |

### 7.3 `arch_contracts` — the gating node

**Purpose.** Nothing downstream can start until persistence, the error model and the
contract-expansion mechanism exist. This node is the reason the graph is not wider at the top.

- **Depends on:** `plan` + plan approval
- **Inputs:** ADR-005/006/007/008/012/017, `contracts/*.yaml`, `database/migrations/001_initial.sql`, `catalog/entities.json`
- **Outputs:**
  - persistence foundation in `apps/api`: TypeORM data source, `DATABASE_URL` wiring, tenant-scoped repository base, transaction helper
  - `database/migrations/002_identity_and_audit.sql` (see §10)
  - RFC 9457 exception filter + `traceId` propagation → `apps/api/src/common/`
  - `zod`-validated environment schema, fail-fast at boot
  - transactional outbox writer + relay skeleton (ADR-012)
  - OpenAPI expansion convention + backward-compatibility diff check in CI
  - `packages/contracts` — types generated from `openapi.yaml`, consumed by `apps/web`
- **Repository paths:** `apps/api/src/common/**`, `apps/api/src/database/**`, `packages/contracts/**`, `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `database/migrations/002_*.sql`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- **Rule IDs:** BR-GEN-002, BR-GEN-003, BR-GEN-004, BR-GEN-005, BR-SEC-008
- **Test layers:** unit (error mapping, env schema), integration (migration forward-apply, tenant repository scoping), contract (OpenAPI diff)
- **Success conditions:** `pnpm check` exits 0 with a *higher* test count; `002` applies forward and re-runs idempotently; a deliberate cross-tenant repository query fails in a test; a deliberate breaking OpenAPI edit fails the CI diff check
- **Retry budget:** 5 iterations, 3 per failure class
- **Rollback:** additive only — drop `002` forward with `003` if needed; no `001` edit
- **Evidence:** command transcripts, migration apply log, new test names
- **Parallelism:** none — this is the join point everything waits on

### 7.4 Milestone node contract

Every `Mx-Sn` slice node carries the same contract. Rather than repeat 22 near-identical
tables, the shared shape is stated once and the per-slice specifics live in §8/§9.

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | one user-observable behaviour, end to end |
| Depends on | listed per slice in §8 |
| Inputs | allocated `BR-*` rules; the OpenAPI/AsyncAPI operations it owns; the handoff screen(s); prior slice outputs |
| Outputs | forward migration (if any) → contract delta → backend module → frontend screen → permissions/audit/metrics → tests → visual evidence |
| Repository paths | `database/migrations/`, `contracts/`, `apps/api/src/<module>/`, `apps/worker/src/`, `apps/web/src/screens/`, `apps/web/src/overlays/`, `apps/web/e2e/` |
| Test layers | unit + integration always; contract when the slice touches `contracts/`; E2E + a11y + visual when it touches a screen; security when it touches tenant-owned data; recovery when it touches jobs or events |
| Success conditions | allocated rules `closed` in `traceability.csv`; `pnpm check` exits 0 **and** test count increased; every required state implemented; visual evidence at 3 viewports; cross-tenant negative test passes |
| Retry budget | 5 iterations / 3 per failure class |
| Rollback | forward migration to reverse schema; feature-flag or revert the frontend route; contract additions are non-breaking by construction |
| Evidence | command transcripts, new test names + counts, screenshots under `evidence/visual/`, audit/log/metric samples |

Every `Mx-GATE` node: depends on all slices in its milestone; runs the §21 per-milestone
gate queries; retry 3; **may not be declared complete by any slice node** (`agent-graph.yaml`
→ `join_requires: all_required_predecessors_terminal_success`).

### 7.5 Parallelism and join conditions

| Can run concurrently | Why it is safe | Join |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `M1-S2` ∥ `M1-S3` | RBAC and audit/error touch different modules; both need `M1-S1`'s identity model | `M1-GATE` |
| `M2-S2` ∥ `M2-S3` ∥ `M2-S4` | lists/tags, custom fields and import are separate modules over a shared recipient model | `M2-GATE` |
| `M3-S3` ∥ `M3-S2` tail | preview consumes the extractor's output but has its own module and screen | `M3-GATE` |
| `M5-S1` early | sender-account CRUD is admin settings; it needs only M1 identity/RBAC. Starting it after `M1-GATE` removes it from the critical path. | `M5-GATE` |
| `M6-S2` early | the notification centre needs only M1 identity + the outbox from `arch_contracts`. Starting it after `M1-GATE` shortens M6. | `M6-GATE` |
| `M7-S1` ∥ `M7-S2` ∥ `M7-S3` ∥ `M7-S4` | quota, security tests, observability and deployment are independent workstreams | `M7-S5` then `packaging` |
| `documentation_final` ∥ `qa_security_final` | docs consume evidence already produced | `completion_gate` |

**Never parallel:** anything that writes `contracts/openapi.yaml` or
`database/migrations/` at the same time as another node. Contract and migration edits
serialise through the owning slice.

**Critical path (longest chain):**

```
intake → discovery → ui_intake → plan → arch_contracts
       → M1-S1 → M1-S2 → M1-GATE
       → M2-S1 → M2-S3 → M2-S4 → M2-GATE
       → M3-S1 → M3-S2 → M3-S3 → M3-GATE
       → M4-S1 → M4-S2 → M4-S3 → M4-S4 → M4-GATE
       → M5-S2 → M5-S3 → M5-S4 → M5-GATE
       → M6-S1 → M6-S3 → M6-GATE
       → M7-{S1|S2|S3|S4} → M7-S5 → M7-GATE
       → packaging → qa_security_final → completion_gate
```

**34 nodes** of the 44 in the graph, machine-verified by the longest-path computation in the
run's validation script. The four M7 slices are interchangeable at that position — they run
in parallel and all four must finish before `M7-S5`, so any one of them sits on the path.

Off the critical path: `ui_intake_register`, `traceability_init`, `M1-S3`, `M2-S2`,
`M3-S3`-parallel work, `M5-S1`, `M6-S2`, three of the four M7 slices, and
`documentation_final`. `M5-S1` and `M6-S2` are the two highest-value early starts —
both unblock after `M1-GATE` and each removes a slice from its milestone's serial chain.

## 8. Milestones and Vertical Slices

Each slice is closed end-to-end in this order:

**business rule → data model/migration → API or event contract → backend → frontend →
permissions/audit/observability → automated tests → visual verification → deployment validation.**

No slice is "backend only". Every slice ends in behaviour a person can see and a test can assert.

### M1 — Foundation, tenant, authentication, RBAC, audit, configuration, error model

Rules: 12 (`BR-GEN-*`, `BR-AUTH-*`, `BR-SEC-002`, `BR-SEC-008`). Tests: `TC-GEN-*`, `TC-AUTH-*` (13).
`BR-GEN-003` and `BR-GEN-005` were reallocated to M5/M2 respectively — see Decision Log DEC-027.

| Slice | Observable behaviour | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **M1-S1** Sign in | A real user signs in at `/login` against PostgreSQL, receives a Secure HttpOnly SameSite session cookie, lands on `/email/compose` in the migrated shell, and signs out. `localStorage["ecs-auth"]` is gone. | `arch_contracts`, `ui_intake_register` |
| **M1-S2** Roles and access | Nav items, routes and actions are gated by role. A Viewer navigating to `/settings/senders` sees the `permission_denied` state, and the API returns RFC 9457 403 for the same call. | M1-S1 |
| **M1-S3** Audit and errors | Every auth and config action writes `audit_log` with `trace_id`. API failures return `application/problem+json`; the UI renders the handoff error vocabulary. Boot fails fast on a missing required env var. | M1-S1 |

### M2 — Recipient, list, tag, custom field, import, bulk update

Rules: 35 (includes `BR-GEN-005`, reallocated from M1 — DEC-027). Tests: `TC-REC-*`, `TC-SEG-*`, `TC-CF-*`, `TC-IMP-*` (47).

| Slice | Observable behaviour | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **M2-S1** Recipient directory | `/recipients` lists, searches, filters and paginates real recipients; add/edit/soft-delete work; duplicate email is rejected per tenant; unsubscribed rows show the correct status. | M1-GATE |
| **M2-S2** Lists and tags | `/recipients/lists` and `/recipients/tags` do full CRUD with server-computed counts; deleting a list used by a scheduled campaign is refused with an explanatory state. | M2-S1 |
| **M2-S3** Custom fields | Admin defines typed custom fields; they appear in the recipient form and in the compose variable panel; system fields cannot be shadowed. | M2-S1 |
| **M2-S4** Import and bulk update | Uploading an XLSX creates an async job; the import screen shows live progress and a final summary; invalid rows download as an error file; a bulk tag/list/custom-data update over a selection runs as an idempotent job with partial-failure reporting. | M2-S1, M2-S3 |

### M3 — Template version, editor, variables, preview, validation

Rules: 9 owned by M3 after DEC-046 and DEC-049; `BR-TPL-001`/`BR-TPL-012` and
`TC-TPL-001`/`TC-TPL-012`/`TC-TPL-015` move to M4-S4 because their literal proof requires a
real campaign snapshot, and `BR-TPL-008`/`TC-TPL-008` move to M4-S3 because their acceptance
blocks schedule/send under campaign policy. Tests owned by M3: 11 `TC-TPL-*` cases.

| Slice | Observable behaviour | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **M3-S1** Template library and versions | `/templates` lists real templates; importing HTML sanitises and stores it; publishing creates an immutable version with a content hash; a published version can never be mutated. Detailed execution: [`M3-S1-TEMPLATES-PLAN.md`](M3-S1-TEMPLATES-PLAN.md). | M2-GATE |
| **M3-S2** Variables | Publishing extracts required and optional variables into `variable_schema_json`; the compose variable panel lists real system + custom variables; unknown or unsafe helpers are rejected. | M3-S1 |
| **M3-S3** Preview and test send | `composePreview` renders server-side with real merge data for a chosen sample recipient, in desktop and mobile, with a deterministic text fallback; `sendTest` delivers one real message to Mailpit. | M3-S2 |

### M4 — Campaign draft, audience query, immutable snapshot, policy validation

Rules: 18 after DEC-044/045/046. Tests: `TC-CMP-*` plus `TC-CF-008`/`TC-CF-013`,
`TC-REC-003`, and `TC-TPL-001`/`TC-TPL-012`/`TC-TPL-015` (22).

| Slice | Observable behaviour | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **M4-S1** Draft | `/email/compose` creates and autosaves a real draft with optimistic concurrency; `/email/drafts` lists real drafts with real completeness. Detailed execution: [`M4-S1-CAMPAIGN-DRAFT-PLAN.md`](M4-S1-CAMPAIGN-DRAFT-PLAN.md). | M3-GATE |
| **M4-S2** Audience | `recipientPicker` resolves lists/tags/exclusions into a deduplicated, eligibility-filtered audience with real counts; over-limit audiences warn. | M4-S1, M2-S2 |
| **M4-S3** Variable policy | `validate-audience` returns the missing-variable matrix; `sendConfirm` blocks send when required variables are missing and offers the accepted resolution path; optional variables fall back. | M4-S2, M3-S2 |
| **M4-S4** Snapshot | Confirming send or schedule freezes audience, template version, merge data, sender and policy result in one transaction; later recipient or template edits cannot change what will be sent; duplicate confirmation is idempotent. | M4-S3 |

### M5 — Scheduling, provider integration, worker, retry, webhook reconciliation

Rules: 25 (includes `BR-GEN-003`, reallocated from M1 — DEC-027). Tests: `TC-SCH-*`, `TC-CFG-*` + the `TC-SEND-*` send subset + `TC-GEN-003` (22).

| Slice | Observable behaviour | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **M5-S1** Sender configuration *(may start after M1-GATE)* | `/settings/senders` does real CRUD; credentials are stored as a secret reference and never returned; "Kiểm tra kết nối" performs a real SMTP probe; `/settings/policy` persists limits that the worker honours. | M1-GATE |
| **M5-S2** Schedule | `scheduleSend` stores UTC + the original IANA zone and enqueues a BullMQ delayed job; a scheduled campaign locks editing; cancel and reschedule work; a late job follows the `send_if_late_within_15_minutes` misfire policy, otherwise marks `missed` and notifies. | M4-GATE, M5-S1 |
| **M5-S3** Send | Confirming send runs the persisted DAG `validate → freeze → partition → send → aggregate`; batches respect the sending policy and provider limits; retries use bounded backoff; suppressed and duplicate recipients are excluded; messages arrive in Mailpit. | M5-S2 |
| **M5-S4** Webhook reconciliation | A signature-verified provider callback updates canonical delivery state; replaying the same event changes nothing; out-of-order events do not regress state. | M5-S3 |

### M6 — Realtime progress, notification centre, history, recovery

Rules: 28 (12 `BR-*` + 16 `BR-NOT-*`). Tests: `TC-HIS-*` + `TC-SEND-*` progress subset (29) **plus authored `BR-NOT-*` tests**.

| Slice | Observable behaviour | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **M6-S1** Live progress | `/history/:campaignId` shows monotonically increasing counters computed from canonical facts, updated by throttled `campaign.progress` events, reconciled by REST on reconnect; a reconnecting state is visible; killing the socket does not lose or corrupt progress. | M5-GATE |
| **M6-S2** Notification centre *(may start after M1-GATE)* | The bell shows a real unread count; the popover lists durable notifications; read state syncs across tabs; deep links re-check permission; the same source event never creates two notifications; going offline and back fills the gap. | M1-GATE |
| **M6-S3** History and recovery | `/history` lists real campaigns with filters; drill-down by status works; partial failures are visible; "Gửi lại" creates a new execution over failed recipients only; export produces a file. | M6-S1 |

### M7 — Quota, security, performance, observability, backup/restore, production readiness

Rules: 8. Tests: `TC-SEC-*` + `TC-CFG-006` (17) + all 10 `DEPLOY-*`.

| Slice | Observable behaviour | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **M7-S1** Quota and limits | Tenant sending quota is enforced; 80/90/100% thresholds emit `quota.threshold_reached` and a notification; exceeding it returns 429 with a Problem body. | M6-GATE |
| **M7-S2** Security hardening | Full security suite green: cross-tenant negatives on every tenant-owned endpoint, RBAC negatives, CSRF, session fixation, webhook signature forgery, template/HTML injection, rate limits. | M6-GATE |
| **M7-S3** Observability *(incremental from M1)* | Pino structured logs with `tenant_id` and `trace_id`; OpenTelemetry traces across API → outbox → job → provider; the metric set in `traceability-plan.yaml`; the runbook alert set defined; a redaction test proves no PII in logs. | M6-GATE |
| **M7-S4** Deployment, backup and restore | `.env` created from the example with generated secrets; `docker compose --env-file .env up -d --build --wait` brings the stack up; `python scripts/smoke_deploy.py` passes; DEPLOY-001…010 all have evidence; a `pg_dump` is taken and restored into an isolated Compose project with RPO/RTO recorded. | M6-GATE |

## 9. Detailed Implementation Steps

Per-slice steps for the first two milestones, which unblock everything else, plus the
pattern that later milestones repeat.

### M1-S1 — Sign in

1. **Migration** `database/migrations/002_identity_and_audit.sql` (delivered by `arch_contracts`,
   consumed here): add `app_user.password_hash`, `app_user.status`, `app_user.last_login_at`;
   create `user_session` (id, tenant_id, user_id, refresh_token_hash, issued_at, expires_at,
   rotated_from, revoked_at, user_agent_hash, ip_hash) and `login_attempt`
   (tenant_id, email, occurred_at, outcome, ip_hash).
2. **Contract** — add to `contracts/openapi.yaml`: `POST /auth/login` (request body,
   204 + `Set-Cookie`, 401, 429), `POST /auth/refresh`, `POST /auth/logout`,
   `GET /auth/me`, `POST /auth/forgot-password`. Regenerate `packages/contracts`.
3. **Backend** `apps/api/src/auth/` — argon2id hashing, session issue and rotation,
   Secure HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookies, CSRF double-submit for state-changing routes,
   `AuthGuard` + `TenantContext` request-scoped provider.
4. **Frontend** — port `LoginScreen` from `app/page.tsx` L80-109 into
   `apps/web/src/screens/auth/LoginScreen.tsx` with identical DOM and class names;
   copy `globals.css`, `ui-icons.tsx` and `public/alta-logo.png`; self-host Geist;
   port the shell (`app/page.tsx` L277-309) into `apps/web/src/app/AppShell.tsx`;
   introduce react-router with the §Routes table; wire login to the real endpoint.
5. **Permissions / audit / observability** — `audit_log` rows for
   `auth.login`, `auth.login_failed`, `auth.logout`, `auth.locked`; `tenant_id` and
   `trace_id` on every log line.
6. **Tests** — unit: hashing, session rotation, lockout counter. integration: login against
   PostgreSQL, cookie flags, cross-tenant login rejection. contract: OpenAPI conformance.
   E2E: sign in → shell → sign out → protected route redirects. a11y: axe on `/login`,
   keyboard-only sign-in. visual: `/login` and the shell at three viewports.
7. **Deployment validation** — `pnpm check` green with a higher test count;
   `docker compose config --quiet` still valid; any new env var added to
   `.env.deploy.example` **and** `docs/deployment/environment-variables.md`.

### M1-S2 — Roles and access

1. Migration `003_rbac.sql`: `role`, `permission`, `role_permission`, `user_role`
   (replacing the free-text `app_user.role`, with a data migration that maps existing values).
2. Contract: add `403` Problem responses to every authenticated operation; add
   `GET /auth/me` permission list.
3. Backend: `PermissionGuard` with declarative decorators; deny-by-default.
4. Frontend: route guards; nav filtering; a `permission_denied` state built from the
   handoff's existing card and status-pill vocabulary.
5. Audit: `rbac.role_assigned`, `rbac.denied`.
6. Tests: RBAC negative matrix (every role × every protected operation);
   E2E Viewer → `/settings/senders` → denied; visual evidence of the denied state.

### M1-S3 — Audit and errors

1. Backend: global RFC 9457 exception filter; `traceId` from the incoming header or generated;
   `zod` env schema validated at boot; audit interceptor.
2. Frontend: a typed Problem client; error surfaces for each migrated screen using the
   handoff's existing error vocabulary.
3. Tests: unit on error mapping; integration asserting `application/problem+json` shape and
   `traceId` propagation into `audit_log`; a boot test proving a missing required env var
   fails fast.

### M2-S1 — Recipient directory

1. Migration `004_recipient_extensions.sql`: `normalized_email` (generated, unique per tenant),
   `deleted_at`, `unsubscribed_at`, search index.
2. Contract: `GET /recipients` (filter, search, cursor pagination), `POST /recipients`,
   `GET|PATCH|DELETE /recipients/{id}`, all with real schemas.
3. Backend: `apps/api/src/recipients/` with tenant-scoped repository, normalisation,
   duplicate rejection, soft delete.
4. Frontend: port `Recipients` (`app/page.tsx` L152-167) `all` view into
   `apps/web/src/screens/recipients/`; port `addRecipient`, `recipientActions`,
   `recipientFilter` overlays; add loading, empty, error and permission_denied states.
5. Audit: `recipient.created|updated|deleted`.
6. Tests: unit on normalisation; integration on the unique constraint and cross-tenant
   isolation; contract; E2E add → search → filter → soft delete; visual at three viewports.

### M3-S1 — Template library and immutable versions

The executable, checkpointed plan is
[`M3-S1-TEMPLATES-PLAN.md`](M3-S1-TEMPLATES-PLAN.md). Its locked delivery sequence is:

1. Repair allocation arithmetic and capture the fresh M2-GATE/OpenAPI/UI/migration baseline.
2. Add forward `015_template_versions.sql` (subject to a start-time collision recheck), the
   mutable template aggregate fields, normalized active-name uniqueness, immutable version
   trigger, content hash, and TypeORM entities.
3. Review and request approval for exact `sanitize-html@2.17.6` and `juice@12.1.2` pins;
   after approval, implement explicit structural
   sanitization → no-network CSS inlining → final sanitization, a 5 MiB limit, deterministic
   reports, and HTTPS-only image resources.
4. Ship tenant-scoped CRUD/archive plus version history using `content:manage`, CSRF, RFC 9457,
   cross-tenant 404 behavior, same-transaction audit, and the template soft-delete evidence
   required by DEC-029.
5. Publish under a row lock into an immutable version with canonical SHA-256 hash, audit action
   `template.published`, and transactional `template.published` outbox row; no UI behavior may
   depend solely on the realtime hint.
6. Add the additive OpenAPI surface and regenerated contract types, then replace `/templates`
   `ComingSoon` with the registered handoff's real grid plus `importHtml`, `templateActions`,
   and `templateFilter` overlays and every required state at three viewports.
7. Close only `BR-TPL-002/006/009/010` in S1. Record prerequisite version evidence for
   `BR-TPL-001/012`; their campaign-snapshot closure is owned by M4-S4 under DEC-046.

### M3-S2 — Variables

The executable, checkpointed plan is
[`M3-S2-VARIABLES-PLAN.md`](M3-S2-VARIABLES-PLAN.md): the locked system catalogue, the
persisted variable schema, the restricted `{{key}}` grammar, and eight checkpoints from
strict-parser RED through live rebuilt-stack probes.

### M4-S1 — Campaign draft

The executable, checkpointed plan is
[`M4-S1-CAMPAIGN-DRAFT-PLAN.md`](M4-S1-CAMPAIGN-DRAFT-PLAN.md). Its locked delivery sequence is:

1. Start the node, and repair the run artifacts that stopped being machine-checked during M3
   (see §19 D-38/D-39).
2. Forward migration `017_campaign_drafts.sql`: draft columns on the published `campaign`
   table, a non-blank-name CHECK for BR-CMP-012 at the database, a known-status CHECK, a
   drafts-list partial index, and a trigger refusing content edits once a campaign is
   `sending`/`completed` (BR-CMP-011 below the service, not only inside it).
3. Pure units first, RED before GREEN: the five-component completeness score the drafts list
   renders, and the BR-CMP-012 assertion that the internal name never reaches subject or body.
4. Real tenant-scoped draft persistence replacing the starter mock, with `ETag`/`If-Match`
   optimistic concurrency (428 absent → 404 unknown → 409 already sending → 412 stale) and a
   duplicate that mints a new id and copies no progress. The existing mock
   `progress`/`schedule`/`send`/`cancel` handlers stay untouched and stay obviously mock.
5. Additive OpenAPI surface plus regenerated contract types, proven additive-only against a
   pre-edit copy.
6. Port the handoff's `Composer` and `Drafts` DOM into real screens with autosave, real
   completeness, the `draftSaved`/`draftActions` overlays and the four required states the
   handoff lacks.
7. Close only `BR-CMP-001/011/012` here. Audience resolution, variable policy and snapshot
   freeze stay with M4-S2/S3/S4; this slice persists an audience *definition* and never
   resolves it.

### M4-S2, M5-S1 and M6-S2 — planned together, executed in parallel

These three nodes are independent in the graph (`M4-S2-audience` depends on M4-S1 and M2-S2;
`M5-S1-sender-config` and `M6-S2-notification-center` depend only on `M1-GATE`), and the human
has asked for them to run concurrently, executed by Codex and reviewed by Claude. Each has its
own executable, checkpointed plan:

- [`M4-S2-AUDIENCE-PLAN.md`](M4-S2-AUDIENCE-PLAN.md) — resolves M4-S1's opaque `audience_json`
  into a deduplicated, eligibility-filtered set with real counts and per-skip reasons; the same
  resolver the send path will use, not an estimate. Closes `BR-SEG-008/009`, `BR-CMP-002/003/009`,
  `BR-REC-003`.
- [`M5-S1-SENDER-CONFIG-PLAN.md`](M5-S1-SENDER-CONFIG-PLAN.md) — real sender identities with
  `secret_ref` (never a plaintext secret column), a real SMTP probe that does not persist a bad
  secret, and the provider-adapter seam ADR-014 requires. Closes `BR-CFG-001/002/003/004/005/007`.
- [`M6-S2-NOTIFICATION-CENTER-PLAN.md`](M6-S2-NOTIFICATION-CENTER-PLAN.md) — replaces the
  in-memory notification mock with durable, per-user, deduplicated persistence where the unread
  API, not the socket, is authoritative. Closes all 16 `BR-NOT-*`, whose tests must be authored
  in-run as `TC-NOT-001…016` because the catalogue contains none.

Their concurrency is governed by [`PARALLEL-EXECUTION-PROTOCOL.md`](PARALLEL-EXECUTION-PROTOCOL.md)
(DEC-055, D-45): reserved migration numbers, reserved `DEC-*`/`D-*` blocks, disjoint OpenAPI path
prefixes with a single late committed edit each, an explicit owner for every shared source file,
and the `inbox/` pattern that keeps three agents out of `state.json`, `traceability.csv`,
`screen-catalog.yaml` and `EXECPLAN.md` simultaneously. Every plan ends at a **visual handoff**:
Codex writes the capture code but never runs, inspects or judges an image, because it cannot —
and M4-S1 proved a green screenshot test is not evidence the right thing rendered (D-42).

### Pattern for M2-S2 … M7-S5

Every remaining slice follows the same seven steps: **migration → contract → backend →
frontend (port the handoff component verbatim, then add the missing required states) →
permissions/audit/observability → tests across the layers named in §7.4 → deployment
validation**. Slice-specific details are captured in the milestone tables in §8 and the
allocation in `traceability-plan.yaml`. Expand a slice's steps in this section when its
node starts, and record what actually happened in §18.

## 10. Data and Migration Plan

`database/migrations/001_initial.sql` is **published**. Its checksum is enforced by
`database/migrate.sh`, and DEPLOY-005 asserts that changing it blocks deployment.
It is never edited. All change is forward-only.

`database/migrate.sh` currently hard-codes `001_initial`. Each new migration requires the
script to be extended to iterate numbered files in order while preserving the per-file
checksum guard — that change is part of `arch_contracts` and is itself covered by DEPLOY-004
and DEPLOY-005.

| # | File | Adds | Slice |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 002 | `002_identity_and_audit.sql` | `app_user.password_hash/status/last_login_at`, `user_session`, `login_attempt`, `idempotency_keys` | arch_contracts / M1-S1 |
| 003 | `003_rbac.sql` | `role`, `permission`, `role_permission`, `user_role` + data migration off `app_user.role` | M1-S2 |
| 004 | `004_recipient_extensions.sql` | `recipient.normalized_email` (unique per tenant), `deleted_at`, `unsubscribed_at`, search indexes | M2-S1 |
| 005 | `005_segments.sql` | `recipient_list_member.source/joined_at`, list/tag count support | M2-S2 |
| 006 | `006_custom_fields.sql` | typed value columns + indexes on `recipient_custom_value`, `validation_json`, `default_json` | M2-S3 |
| 007 | `007_async_jobs.sql` | `async_jobs`, `job_items` (import, bulk, export, reconcile) | M2-S4 |
| 015 | `015_template_versions.sql` | Mutable draft fields/lifecycle/normalized active-name uniqueness on `email_template`; `email_template_version.variable_schema_json`, `content_hash`, `published_at`; publish immutability trigger | M3-S1 |
| next free at node start | `*_campaign_audience.sql` | `campaign_audience_rules` | M4-S2 |
| 022 | `022_campaign_snapshot.sql` | `campaign_snapshot.variable_schema_json/total_snapshot/sendable_count/skipped_count/frozen_by/superseded_at` + partial unique index (one live snapshot per campaign); `campaign_recipient.snapshot_id/email_snapshot/eligibility/skipped_reason` + per-snapshot unique index; snapshot and recipient immutability triggers; `campaign_no_edit_after_send()` extended to guard `queued/validating/scheduled` (DEC-082, DEC-085) | M4-S4 |
| 023 | `023_campaign_snapshot_hardening.sql` | Composite tenant-scoped FKs on `campaign_snapshot`/`campaign_recipient`; `id`/`tenant_id`/`frozen_by` guards on the snapshot immutability trigger, `id`/`tenant_id` on the recipient one; `campaign_snapshot_counts_valid` CHECK. Preserved from an abandoned worktree (DEC-087), landed by `M5-S2` CP1a (D-79/D-80/D-81) | M4-S4 (authored) / M5-S2 (landed) |
| 024 | `024_campaign_schedule.sql` | Widens `campaign_status_known` to add `missed`/`blocked`; `actual_started_at`/`delay_seconds` + non-negative CHECK; `campaign_schedule_complete` CHECK (both schedule columns or neither); `idx_campaign_due_schedule` partial index; reconciles `campaign_no_edit_after_send()` so the schedule columns are writable only on `draft->scheduled` and clearable only on `scheduled->{cancelled,missed}` (D-80, D-82) | M5-S2 |
| 025 | `025_campaign_dispatch_scan.sql` | `due_scheduled_campaigns()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function (same shape as `013_outbox_relay_api.sql`'s relay function) so the dispatcher's cross-tenant due-campaign scan is not refused by RLS (D-83) | M5-S2 |
| next free at node start | `*_sender_accounts.sql` | `sender_accounts` (provider, from/reply-to, `secret_ref`, status, rate_limit), `sending_policy` | M5-S1 |
| 026 | `026_campaign_execution.sql` | `campaign_recipient_status_known`/`campaign_recipient_skipped_status_agrees` CHECKs; `campaign_recipient.execution_id/batch_no/attempt_count/next_retry_at/claimed_at/content_hash`; `campaign_execution` (DAG state per snapshot) and `message_attempt` (one row per send attempt, idempotency-keyed) tables; `sender_config.rate_limit_per_minute/daily_send_limit`; `sending_policy.batch_size/max_attempts/tenant_rate_limit_per_minute`; `recipient.suppressed_at/suppression_reason`; `queued_campaign_executions()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` cross-tenant scan function (same shape as `025`'s `due_scheduled_campaigns()`) | M5-S3 |
| 027 | `027_delivery_events.sql` | `delivery_event` (append-only ledger, `UNIQUE (provider, provider_event_id)` as the idempotency key, `event_type`/`outcome` CHECK vocabulary, `eow_app` granted `SELECT, INSERT` only); `campaign_recipient.delivery_state_at` (the ordering high-water mark A7 needs -- the placeholder's dedupe-only key could not by itself guarantee out-of-order events do not regress state, D-105's sibling finding SS0(g)); `idx_message_attempt_provider_message_id` (D-107); `resolve_provider_message()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` tenant/recipient resolver (same shape as `013`/`025`/`026`'s scan functions), `DISTINCT`-selecting so a genuinely ambiguous mapping across two recipients returns 2 rows while a crash-retry duplicate for one recipient (D-107, M5-S3 R2) collapses to 1 | M5-S4 |
| 028 | `028_progress_counters.sql` | `campaign_execution.progress_seq` (D-116/DEC-122's monotonic dedupe cursor) + nine per-status `*_count` columns (the denormalised summary `campaign.progress` publishes and `progress-reconcile.ts` repairs) + `reconciled_at`; `campaign_execution_counts_non_negative` CHECK; `reconcilable_campaign_executions()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` cross-tenant scan (same shape as `013`/`025`/`026`/`027`'s) bounded to `status='sending' OR finished_at > now() - 7 days` | M6-S1 |
| next free at node start | `*_notification_prefs.sql` | notification preferences, action state, dedupe key, message key + params | M6-S2 |
| next free at node start | `*_quota.sql` | tenant limits and quota accounting | M7-S1 |

**Invariants enforced by migration and test:** every tenant-owned table carries `tenant_id`;
published template versions and campaign snapshots are immutable (trigger-enforced);
`delivery_events` and `idempotency_keys` are uniquely constrained so replays are no-ops;
all timestamps are `timestamptz` in UTC with the original IANA zone retained alongside.

**Rollback:** forward-only. A defective migration is reversed by the next numbered migration.
Every migration is tested for forward apply *and* for idempotent re-run before its slice closes.

## 11. API and Realtime Contract Plan

### HTTP

`contracts/openapi.yaml` is the source of truth and today it is a stub. It grows
**additively**, one delta per slice. Rules:

- Adding a path, operation, property or response is allowed at any time.
- Removing or narrowing anything requires a **superseding ADR** (AGENTS.md §5).
- Every mutating operation accepts an idempotency key (`BR-GEN-005`) and every
  tenant-owned operation documents `403` and `404` as Problem responses.
- CI runs a backward-compatibility diff against the previous milestone tag; a deliberate
  breaking edit must fail that check before `arch_contracts` closes.
- `packages/contracts` generates TypeScript types from the spec; `apps/web` imports only
  generated types, never hand-written DTOs (ADR-005).

Target surface by milestone, reconciled against `catalog/api-baseline.json`:

| Milestone | Operations added |
| --- | --- |
| M1 | `auth/login`, `auth/refresh`, `auth/logout`, `auth/me`, `auth/forgot-password` |
| M2 | `recipients` (+`{id}`), `recipients/bulk-jobs`, `recipients/imports`, `imports/{jobId}`, `lists`, `tags`, `custom-fields` |
| M3 | `templates`, `templates/{id}/publish`, `template-versions/{id}/preview`, `template-versions/{id}/test-send` |
| M4 | `campaigns` (+`{id}` PATCH), `campaigns/{id}/audience-preview`, `templates/{id}/validate-audience` (schema), `campaigns/{id}/send` (schema) |
| M5 | `sender-accounts` (+`{id}`, `/test`), `settings/sending-policy`, `campaigns/{id}/schedule` (schema), `webhooks/providers/{provider}` |
| M6 | `notifications` (schema), `notifications/{id}/read`, `notifications/read-all`, `history`, `history/{id}/recipients`, `campaigns/{id}/pause`, `/resume`, `/resend` |
| M7 | `audit-logs`, quota surfaces |

`GET /campaigns/{id}/events` from `api-baseline.json` is **not** implemented — see DEC-004.

### Realtime

`contracts/asyncapi.yaml` defines 20 channels sharing one `EventEnvelope`
(`event_id`, `event_type`, `occurred_at`, `tenant_id`, `aggregate_id`, `version`, `trace_id`, `data`).
`catalog/realtime-events.json` supplies each event's channel pattern, cadence and dedupe key —
treat the two together as the realtime contract.

Non-negotiable behaviours (ADR-009/010/012/016):

1. Events are **hints**, never the only copy of business state. REST + PostgreSQL are canonical.
2. Every event is server-originated, tenant-authorised at the room level, versioned and
   deduplicated by `event_id`.
3. Publication happens **after** the domain transaction commits, via the transactional outbox.
4. `campaign.progress` is throttled to at most 1/second or 250 recipients, whichever is quieter.
5. Counters are monotonic and derived from `campaign_recipient` facts.
6. On reconnect the client refetches by version/cursor; `rt.resync_required` forces it.
7. The UI never accepts a completion event until REST agrees.
8. **`apps/web/src/App.tsx` must stop listening for `campaign.progress.updated`** — the
   contract event is `campaign.progress` (DRIFT-01, fixed in M6-S1).

Contract tests validate every emitted envelope against the AsyncAPI schema, and assert that
each event's dedupe key matches `catalog/realtime-events.json`.

---

## 12. UI Migration and Visual Verification Plan

Full inventory: `ui-inventory.yaml`. Screen registry: `screen-catalog.yaml` (created by `traceability_init`).

### Strategy

The handoff's `globals.css` (187 KB) is keyed entirely on **semantic class names**
(`.app-shell`, `.compose-grid`, `.config-master-detail`, `.history-table`, …). Therefore:

> Port the DOM structure and class names verbatim into `apps/web` React components, and copy
> `globals.css` unchanged. Visual equivalence then holds by construction, and any diff is a
> genuine defect rather than a re-implementation artefact.

Concretely: split `app/page.tsx` and `app/action-overlays.tsx` by screen and overlay into
`apps/web/src/screens/**` and `apps/web/src/overlays/**` as each milestone claims them.
Add `@tailwindcss/postcss` (required by `@import "tailwindcss"`). Self-host Geist and
Geist Mono under `apps/web/public/fonts` with the same `--font-geist-sans` /
`--font-geist-mono` variable names so `globals.css` needs no edit.

**Incremental only.** `apps/web` must build and pass `pnpm check` after every slice.
`apps/web` is never replaced wholesale. Approved screens are never redesigned.

### Routing

The handoff is one Next.js page driven by React state; nothing is addressable. `apps/web`
already depends on `react-router-dom` 7. Real routes are **required**, not a redesign:
`BR-NOT-010` (stable deep link per actionable notification), `BR-HIS-003` (drill-down) and
`BR-AUTH-004` (per-route permission control) cannot be satisfied by in-memory view state.
Route table: `ui-inventory.yaml` → `routing.routes`. See DEC-003.

### Required states

`design-reference/ui-source-contract.yaml` requires **loading, empty, error, success,
permission_denied** and **reconnecting** (where realtime applies). The handoff ships
`success` and a login-only `error`. The other four are absent everywhere.

Adding them is **contract compliance, not redesign** — but each must be built from the
handoff's existing visual vocabulary: the same card geometry for skeletons, the existing
`status` pill variants, the existing colour tokens. No new visual language is invented.

### Mocks to replace

`ui-inventory.yaml` catalogues them with IDs (`MOCK-AUTH-01` … `MOCK-HIS-05`). The classes are:
data literals (`people`, `drafts`, `histories`, `recipientLists`, `recipientTags`,
`recipientImports`, `configs`, `notificationItems`), ~20 mutations that resolve with a toast,
simulated progress, simulated notifications, a fake schedule, a hardcoded `Administrator`
profile, and `localStorage["ecs-auth"]` standing in for a session. Each is replaced per
`design-reference/ui-source-contract.yaml` → `mock_replacements`. The theme and sidebar
preferences stay in `localStorage` — those are legitimate UI state.

### Visual verification

Per `design-reference/visual-acceptance.md`, capture handoff baseline and production render
at **1440×900**, **768×1024** and **390×844** for every screen and every required state.
Store under `evidence/visual/` in the run directory — never in `design-reference/`.
Playwright drives capture; every material difference gets a written explanation in
`screen-catalog.yaml` → `diff_notes`.

Two observations to carry forward:
- The handoff breakpoints are 1100px and 760px, so the 768px acceptance viewport renders the
  **desktop** grid. That is the baseline and must be reproduced, not "fixed" (A-10).
- The mobile viewport (390px) is below 760px, so the sidebar becomes a fixed 76px icon rail
  with hidden labels. Verify the icon rail is keyboard-reachable and labelled.

---

## 13. Security and Tenant-Isolation Plan

Target: `docs/operations/security-baseline.md`. Current state: **only CORS is configured.**
No authentication, no authorisation, no CSRF, no rate limiting, no input validation, no
tenant guard.

| Control | Where | Slice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Secure HttpOnly SameSite session cookies, rotated refresh | `apps/api/src/auth/` | M1-S1 |
| CSRF double-submit on state-changing routes | `apps/api/src/common/` | M1-S1 |
| Login lockout and throttling | `apps/api/src/auth/` | M1-S1 |
| Server-side RBAC, deny-by-default | `apps/api/src/common/guards/` | M1-S2 |
| `TenantContext` + tenant-scoped repository base | `apps/api/src/database/` | arch_contracts |
| Input validation on every endpoint (`zod`) | per module | every slice |
| Parameterised queries only | repository layer | arch_contracts |
| HTML sanitisation + CSS inlining for templates | `apps/api/src/templates/` | M3-S1 |
| Strict Handlebars — whitelisted helpers, no arbitrary execution | `apps/api/src/templates/` | M3-S2 |
| SMTP credentials stored as `secret_ref`, never returned or logged | `apps/api/src/senders/` | M5-S1 |
| Signed webhook verification + replay dedupe | `apps/api/src/webhooks/` | M5-S4 |
| Realtime room authorisation per tenant and resource | `apps/api/src/realtime/` | M6-S1 |
| Rate limits and quota enforcement | `apps/api/src/common/`, worker | M7-S1 |
| PII redaction in logs, metrics and traces | logger config | M7-S3 |

**Tenant isolation is tested, not assumed.** Every tenant-owned endpoint gets a cross-tenant
negative test: authenticate as tenant A, request a tenant B resource, assert 403/404 and
assert nothing leaked in the Problem body. This is a gate condition at every milestone, not
a M7 activity. `arch_contracts` lands the first one against the repository base so the
pattern exists from the start.

---

## 14. Observability and Audit Plan

Target: ADR-017 and `docs/operations/runbook.md`. Current state: **nothing** — Nest's default
console logger, no tracing, no metrics, no correlation IDs.

- **Logs** — Pino structured JSON. Mandatory fields: `trace_id`, `tenant_id`, `actor_id`,
  `module`, `action`. Forbidden: session tokens, SMTP credentials, recipient custom values,
  email bodies. A redaction test enforces this (M7-S3).
- **Traces** — OpenTelemetry. `trace_id` propagates request → transaction → outbox row →
  job → provider call → webhook, so a duplicate, delay or retry can be explained end to end.
- **Metrics** — the set enumerated in `traceability-plan.yaml` → `runtime_evidence`, including
  `eow_send_attempt_total{result}`, `eow_send_retry_total`, `eow_progress_reconcile_drift`,
  `eow_schedule_misfire_total`, `eow_quota_usage_ratio`, `eow_idempotent_replay_total`.
- **Audit** — `audit_log` rows for authentication, role change, sender change, template
  publish, audience resolution, schedule/send/cancel, bulk update and export, with masked diffs.
- **Alerts** — queue age, failed-job rate, provider rejection rate, webhook lag, progress
  reconciliation drift, notification delivery failure (runbook). Defined in M7-S3.

Observability is built **incrementally from M1**, not bolted on in M7. M7-S3 completes and
verifies it; each earlier slice adds its own audit actions and metrics as part of step 5.

---

## 15. Test Strategy

Layers, ownership and tooling: `traceability-plan.yaml` → `test_layers`.
Allocation of all 159 catalogued cases: `traceability-plan.yaml` → `allocation`.

Rules that govern every slice:

1. **Never weaken a test to make it pass** (`.agents/agent-loop.yaml` → `forbidden`).
   If a test is wrong, fix the test and record why in the Decision Log.
2. **`--passWithNoTests` is not evidence.** Every gate asserts a test *count* that increased.
3. **Every tenant-owned endpoint has a cross-tenant negative test.** No exceptions.
4. **Every P0 rule has at least one automated test** before its milestone gate closes.
   P1 and P2 may be waived only with a Decision Log entry naming a compensating control.
5. **Contract tests are two-directional**: the implementation conforms to the spec, and the
   spec has not broken backward compatibility.
6. **Recovery is a first-class layer**: retry, duplicate webhook, duplicate job, Redis
   reconnect, worker crash mid-batch, schedule misfire, reconciliation drift.
7. **`BR-NOT-*` tests must be authored** — `catalog/test-cases.json` has no `TC-NOT-*` prefix
   (A-09). New IDs are recorded in `traceability.csv` as `authored-in-run`.

Release gate (`docs/testing/test-strategy.md`): all P0 automated tests pass, no open critical
vulnerability, migrations tested forward and the restore path rehearsed, progress counters
reconcile to recipient facts.

---

## 16. Deployment and Operations Plan

ADR-024 is the contract: one `compose.yaml`, multi-stage images, a documented `.env`, and

```bash
docker compose --env-file .env up -d --build --wait
```

brings up PostgreSQL → Redis → one-shot checksum-guarded migration → API → worker →
scheduler → Nginx-served web → Mailpit, in health-gated order.

**Standing obligation on every slice** (AGENTS.md §2): any change to runtime dependencies,
ports, environment variables, migrations, startup, health/readiness or static assets must
update `compose.yaml`, `.env.deploy.example`, the Dockerfile targets and
`docs/deployment/environment-variables.md` **together**, then re-validate Compose.

`database/migrate.sh` must be generalised to iterate numbered migrations while keeping the
per-file checksum guard (§10). DEPLOY-004 and DEPLOY-005 cover the result.

**M7-S4 produces the deployment evidence:** create `.env` from `.env.deploy.example` with
locally generated secrets (never committed, never printed into this plan), boot the stack,
run `python scripts/smoke_deploy.py`, and record evidence for DEPLOY-001 through DEPLOY-010.
DEPLOY-001 already has passing evidence from the planning baseline (§4.1).

**Production deployment remains a STOP gate.** Local packaging verification is autonomous;
shipping to a production host is not.

---

## 17. Rollback and Recovery

| Layer | Mechanism |
| --- | --- |
| Schema | Forward-only. A defective migration is reversed by the next numbered migration. Never edit a published file. |
| API contract | Additive only. A breaking change needs a superseding ADR and a version strategy (`BR-SEC-008`). |
| Frontend | Each screen migrates behind its own route. A defective screen is reverted to the previous route component without touching the rest of `apps/web`. |
| Campaign execution | Every DAG node is idempotent. Re-running `validate`, `freeze`, `send-batch`, `aggregate` or `reconcile` is safe. Cancel is honoured until first provider submission; after that it is best-effort for unsent recipients (runbook). |
| Jobs and events | Idempotency keys, outbox `UNIQUE (aggregate_type, aggregate_id, aggregate_version, event_type)`, and `delivery_events` `UNIQUE (provider, provider_event_id)` make replay a no-op. |
| Progress | Counters are recomputed from `campaign_recipient` facts. `eow_progress_reconcile_drift` must be 0 at completion. |
| Data | `pg_dump -Fc` per `docs/deployment/operations.md`. Restore is deliberately manual; M7-S4 rehearses it in an isolated Compose project and records RPO/RTO. |
| Deployment | `docker compose down` retains volumes. `--volumes` is destructive and is an explicit operator action, never an agent action. |
| Agent run | `state.json` is the durable checkpoint. A replacement agent validates the approval scope, reads completed-node evidence, re-checks the workspace and resumes the first non-terminal node. |

---

### M2-S1-recipients — what changed

| Area | Change | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `database/migrations/006_recipient_extensions.sql` | New forward migration | The `recipient` table already existed since `001_initial.sql`; this slice adds `normalized_email` (STORED generated column), `deleted_at`, `unsubscribed_at`, `custom_data` jsonb placeholder, a CHECK constraint on `subscription_status`, replaces the case-sensitive `UNIQUE(tenant_id,email)` with a partial unique index on `(tenant_id, normalized_email) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` (BR-REC-001, race-free at the DB level, and lets a soft-deleted recipient's email be reused), and seeds+grants two new permission rows |
| `apps/api/src/common/permissions.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/nav.ts` | Added `recipient:read`/`recipient:manage`; `/recipients` moved off the coarser `content:manage` | Recipients now have real CRUD, matching `campaign:read`/`campaign:manage`'s granularity |
| `apps/api/src/database/tenant-scoped.repository.ts` | `repository`/`tenantId` fields widened `private` → `protected` | So a subclass (`RecipientsRepository`) can build custom tenant-scoped `createQueryBuilder` queries the base class's `find()`/`save()` don't cover (search, keyset pagination, soft delete) |
| `apps/api/src/recipients/**` | New module | `RecipientsRepository` (plain class, not DI-managed — see the DI-ordering bug below), `RecipientsService` (singleton, tenantId passed explicitly per call), `RecipientsController` (`@RequirePermission`/`@AuditLog` on every route, zod-validated DTOs) |
| `apps/api/src/auth/tenant-context.ts` | Comment-only: documented a "KNOWN HAZARD" | See the DI-ordering bug below — a real, reproduced bug this node found and worked around, not fixed at the root |
| `contracts/openapi.yaml` | Replaced the arch_contracts-era `/recipients` stub with real schemas; added `GET/PATCH/DELETE /recipients/{recipientId}` | The stub had bare 200/201 descriptions and no request/response bodies |
| `apps/web/src/screens/recipients/RecipientsScreen.tsx`, `apps/web/src/overlays/{AddRecipientOverlay,RecipientActionsOverlay,RecipientFilterOverlay}.tsx` | New | DOM/class names ported verbatim from the handoff's `Recipients` "all" view and its `addRecipient`/`recipientActions`/`recipientFilter` overlays; wired to the real API. Establishes `apps/web/src/overlays/` as the overlay-component convention (first consumer) |
| `apps/web/src/app/AppRoutes.tsx` | `/recipients` now renders `RecipientsScreen` instead of `ComingSoon` | |
| `apps/api/test/integration/{rbac-matrix,permissions-service}.test.ts` | Edited: hardcoded exact-permission-list fixtures updated | Adaptation to the two new permission keys, not new bugs — same category as M1-S2's `AuthGuard` constructor-signature fixture updates |

**Real bugs found and fixed (not just found and documented):**

1. **NestJS DI-ordering hazard (new discovery — `TenantContext` existed since M1-S1 but had zero real consumers until this node).** Constructor-injecting the REQUEST-scoped `TenantContext` into another REQUEST-scoped provider caused every `/recipients` route to 401 (`Not authenticated.`) for a genuinely valid session — reproduced live via `curl` (identical cookie succeeded on `GET /auth/me`, 200, but 401'd on `GET /recipients`) and confirmed via temporary debug logging that `TenantContext.require()` ran with `request.auth` still `undefined`, before the global `AuthGuard` had populated it. Root cause not fully bisected (documented as a "KNOWN HAZARD" in `tenant-context.ts`'s class comment for future modules). Fixed by making `RecipientsRepository` a plain, non-DI class constructed per call with an explicit `tenantId` parameter, and `RecipientsService` a singleton whose every method takes `tenantId` explicitly (read by the controller from the already-guarded `request.auth.tenantId`).
2. **`@AuditLog` crash on the failure path.** `resolveEntityId` for `POST /recipients` assumed `result` was always the created entity, but `AuditInterceptor` calls it with `result=undefined` on failure (e.g. the 409 duplicate-email case) — crashed with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'id')`, masking the real 409 as a generic 500 to the client. Caught live via the E2E duplicate-email assertion. Fixed with optional chaining (create) and by reading the id from `request.params` instead of `result` (update, always available on both success and failure).
3. **Two real accessibility bugs**, found via axe on `/recipients`: table checkboxes with no accessible name (`aria-label` added); an empty trailing `<th>` for the actions column (`empty-table-header`, fixed with a visually-hidden "Hành động" span).

**Traceability decisions (DEC-030/DEC-031, see §20):** BR-SEG-010's acceptance text is about list/tag pagination, which does not exist as a real feature until `M2-S2-lists-tags` — reallocated slice (not milestone; still M2 in `traceability-plan.yaml`) to `M2-S2`, left `not_started`. BR-REC-005/006 (list/tag membership) and BR-REC-009 (bulk actions) name features that don't exist until `M2-S2`/`M2-S4` respectively — same treatment. BR-REC-003's *statement* (the 4-state model, only `active` eligible to send) is closed for real (DB CHECK constraint, tested); its acceptance detail ("Audience preview phân loại...") names a screen that only exists in M4's compose/audience flow — left `not_started` with `slice` split to `M2-S1 (data model) / M4 (audience preview UI)` rather than falsely closed, same honesty standard as DEC-027/029.

Verification: `pnpm run check` — 6/6 typecheck; tests apps/api 102 + `runtime-orchestration` 3 + apps/web 5 = 110 (up from the 92-test M1-GATE baseline); 6/6 build. `apps/web/e2e` full suite (auth+rbac+errors+recipients) 16/16 live against a real compiled API + Vite dev server + dockerized PostgreSQL. Migration `006` applied and re-verified idempotent. `docker compose --env-file .env config --quiet` exits 0, no new env var. `validate_plan.py` 0 errors, 0 warnings.

### M2-S3-custom-fields — what changed

Numbered steps actually followed (§9 pattern for M2-S2…M7-S5, expanded here):

1. **Discovery before touching code.** Read `M2-S1-recipients`'s full evidence, `catalog/ba-rules.json` BR-CF-001..009, `catalog/entities.json`'s `custom_field_definitions`/`recipient_custom_values` shapes, and `database/migrations/001_initial.sql` in full — found that `custom_field_definition` (`id, tenant_id, field_key, label, data_type, required, UNIQUE(tenant_id, field_key)`) and `recipient_custom_value` (`tenant_id, recipient_id, field_id -> custom_field_definition, value_json jsonb`) **already exist in the published baseline**, contradicting `006_recipient_extensions.sql`'s own comment (which described `recipient.custom_data jsonb` as a from-scratch placeholder) and this node's own task brief (which assumed no normalized table existed). See DEC-033.
2. **Migration** `database/migrations/007_custom_fields.sql`: forward-*alters* the pre-existing `custom_field_definition` table (same pattern `006` used on `recipient`) — adds `default_value`/`enum_options` jsonb, `sensitive` boolean, `created_at`/`updated_at`, and three CHECK constraints (key format `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, `data_type IN (...)`, reserved-key rejection). Applied and re-verified idempotent against real local PostgreSQL. Value storage decision: recipient-level custom-data values are stored in `recipient.custom_data` jsonb (M2-S1's existing placeholder and API contract), *not* the pre-existing `recipient_custom_value` table — see DEC-033.
3. **Contract**: `GET/POST /custom-fields`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /custom-fields/{id}` with real schemas; a new `CustomFieldReservedKeyProblem` response (additive) carrying `reservedKeys: string[]` for BR-CF-003's 422. Found and fixed a real, pre-existing gap while wiring this: `apps/api/src/common/problem.ts`'s `mapErrorToProblem` only ever emitted the standard `type/title/status/detail/traceId` fields, silently dropping any structured extension data a controller's exception body carried (e.g. this rule's `reservedKeys`, and — previously unnoticed — M2-S1's own `recipientId` on a 409). Fixed by spreading non-standard fields from the exception response onto the Problem body (`problem.ts`'s new `extensionsFrom` helper), unit-tested, zero change to the passing shape for exceptions that carry only `message`.
4. **Backend** `apps/api/src/custom-fields/`: `CustomFieldsRepository` (plain class, not DI-managed — same `RecipientsRepository` pattern from M2-S1, same DI-ordering hazard avoided), `CustomFieldsService` (reserved-key 422, duplicate-key 409, key-immutable-on-update via a `.strict()` zod schema that has no `key` property at all), `CustomFieldsController` (`recipient:read` for GET, `settings:manage` for mutations — an admin schema-defining action, not per-recipient CRUD). `apps/api/src/custom-fields/custom-field-values.ts`: `coerceCustomFieldValue`/`validateCustomData`/`applyCustomFieldDefaults` — BR-CF-002's actual type validation/coercion, exported and reused by `RecipientsService.create`/`update`, which now reject an unknown or wrong-typed `customData` key (400) instead of silently persisting an opaque JSON blob.
5. **Frontend**: `CustomFieldsScreen.tsx` (new admin screen, `/settings/custom-fields`, `settings:manage`-gated) + `ManageCustomFieldOverlay.tsx` — no handoff screen owns this destination (see DEC-034), built from the existing `workspace-module-frame`/`module-card`/`table-wrap`/`action-overlay` vocabulary, same precedent as M1-S2's `PermissionDeniedScreen`. `AddRecipientOverlay.tsx`/`RecipientActionsOverlay.tsx` both gained a real "Trường tùy chỉnh" section (step 3) rendering one typed input per admin-defined field, wired to `customData` — the literal EXECPLAN §8 acceptance detail. The "compose variable panel" half of §8's acceptance is not built (compose is M4 scope) — `GET /custom-fields` is the real API a future M4 node will consume; not stubbed.
6. **Permissions/audit/observability**: `@AuditLog` on `custom-field.created|updated|deleted` (existing M1-S3 convention, no new mechanism). BR-CF-009 additionally required per-*value* audit (not just per-definition): `RecipientsService` now writes one `recipient.custom_data.updated` audit row per changed field key (before/after, masked `"***"` when the field is flagged `sensitive`) whenever a recipient's `customData` changes — field-key granularity, not one row for the whole blob, so it is queryable "theo recipient" at the field level the rule's acceptance text requires. `sourceJob` is `null` here (direct API writes); M2-S4's bulk-update job is expected to pass its own job id through the same hook.
7. **Tests (TDD)**: unit — `custom-field.dto.test.ts` (12), `custom-field-values.test.ts` (16), extended `problem.test.ts` (+2) — written and watched RED before the corresponding implementation existed. Integration — `apps/api/test/integration/custom-fields.test.ts` (17, real dockerized PostgreSQL): uniqueness, cross-tenant isolation, reserved-key 422 (app + DB CHECK, defence in depth), key-format DB CHECK, label-only update preserving key, type coercion/rejection per declared type, unknown-key rejection, reserved-key-in-customData rejection, BR-CF-009 audit round-trip incl. the sensitive-masking case, required-field default application, enum-option validation. `rbac-matrix.test.ts` extended with `GET`/`POST /custom-fields` (admin+operator vs admin-only). Contract — `openapi-compat-check.mjs` self-check + its own test suite re-run. E2E — `apps/web/e2e/custom-fields.spec.ts`: create a field on the admin screen → BR-CF-003 reserved-key rejection shown in the overlay → field appears in the recipient add form → typed value saved → round-trips in the edit form; plus an axe scan. Full suite re-run live: 51 Playwright specs, 48 pass (the 3 failures are the pre-existing handoff-baseline-server-dependent tests unrelated to this node, same gap M1-S1 already disclosed).
8. **Visual evidence**: `apps/web/e2e/visual-capture.spec.ts` extended with a new M2-S3 block (9 tests) — custom-fields admin success/empty, and the extended recipient form showing the "Trường tùy chỉnh" section — at 1440×900/768×1024/390×844, saved under `evidence/visual/M2-S3-custom-fields/production/` (9 PNGs, 53KB-146KB, visually reviewed: table renders correctly with all 5 columns, recipient form's step-3 section renders the typed inputs correctly). No handoff baseline exists for any of these (screen absent from the handoff, per DEC-034). `screen-catalog.yaml` gets a new `UI-CF-001` entry and an addendum to `UI-REC-001`'s `diff_notes`.
9. **Deployment validation**: `pnpm run check` — 6/6 typecheck; tests apps/api 155 + `runtime-orchestration` 3 + apps/web 5 = **163** (up from the 110-test M2-S1 baseline); 6/6 build. `docker compose --env-file .env config --quiet` exits 0, no new env var. Migration `007` applied and re-verified idempotent (twice).

**Real bugs found and fixed (not just found and documented):**

1. **Pre-existing normalized custom-field tables, undiscovered until this node.** `001_initial.sql` already models `custom_field_definition`/`recipient_custom_value`, contradicting both `006`'s own comment and this node's task brief. Extended the existing table via a forward migration instead of creating a duplicate (which would have hard-failed at apply time — and did, on the first attempt, with `relation "custom_field_definition" already exists`, caught immediately rather than silently). See DEC-033.
2. **RFC 9457 Problem responses silently dropped structured exception data.** `mapErrorToProblem` only ever surfaced `message` as `detail`, discarding any other field a controller's exception body carried — meaning M2-S1's own `409` `recipientId` field was *never actually reaching a real HTTP client*, only visible to a test calling the service directly. Fixed generically (`extensionsFrom`), benefiting every existing structured-exception endpoint, not just this node's own `reservedKeys`.
3. **`CustomFieldsService.update()` returned a partial entity.** TypeORM's `save()` on a partial object returns only the properties actually passed to it, not a re-fetch of the full row — an update touching only `label` returned an object where `fieldKey` was `undefined`. Caught by this node's own TDD (a test asserting `updated.fieldKey === created.fieldKey` failed with `undefined`, not a wrong value). Fixed by reloading via `getOrThrow` after `save()`.
4. **`audit_log`'s immutability trigger (M1-S3) makes a tenant fixture undeletable once it owns an audit row.** This node's own new `recipient.custom_data.updated` audit-writing test is the first suite to both write audit rows *and* attempt to delete its own tenant fixture in `afterAll` in the same run — `recipients.test.ts` never wrote audit rows, so this never surfaced before. Not a defect (the trigger is working as designed for BR-SEC-002); documented in the test file and left as two harmless orphaned test tenants per run, consistent with this run's disclosed-not-hidden evidence discipline.

**Traceability decision (DEC-032, see §20):** `BR-CF-005`'s acceptance text ("Job lưu recipient snapshot/selection token; count processed không vượt resolved_count") is about bulk-update job scope resolution, a feature that does not exist until `M2-S4-import-bulk` — reallocated **slice** (still `M2` milestone in `traceability-plan.yaml`, already correct there) from `M2-S3-custom-fields` to `M2-S4-import-bulk` in both `state.json` and `traceability.csv`, left `not_started`. Same "acceptance names a surface that doesn't exist yet" shape as DEC-027/029/030/031.
