Recorded as a real, current gap rather than silently worked around, in the same spirit as D-22's "flagged for a dedicated tooling node": a future milestone or a standalone tooling node should either add a Vite dev proxy for `/api` (matching what the production nginx/Docker path already does) or standardise every fixture helper on the app's own absolute-URL API clients instead of ad hoc relative fetches, and should document how/when the handoff preview server is expected to be running for its one consumer test. |
| D-45 | Planning `M4-S2`, `M5-S1` and `M6-S2` for concurrent execution surfaced that the three collide on far more than the obvious: the next free **migration number** (all three would independently claim 019), `contracts/openapi.yaml` (all three add paths), `migrations.lock.json`, `app.module.ts`, `permissions.ts`, `visual-capture.spec.ts`, and all four run artifacts (`state.json`, `traceability.csv`, `screen-catalog.yaml`, `EXECPLAN.md`) which every node writes at every checkpoint. On a single shared working tree none of this is git-mergeable — there is only last-writer-wins, and M4-S1 already produced two live instances of the class (a tree changing under a reviewing agent mid-analysis; a full test suite competing for CPU with an active implementing agent, which is exactly what D-40's flake is sensitive to). | Wrote `PARALLEL-EXECUTION-PROTOCOL.md` (DEC-055) before any of the three plans: reserved migration numbers 019/020/021 with a `+30` overflow rule, reserved `DEC-*`/`D-*` blocks per node, disjoint OpenAPI path prefixes each edited once and committed immediately, a named single owner for every contended source file, and an `inbox/<node-id>.md` pattern that keeps Codex out of the four shared run artifacts entirely (Claude merges them at review). It also forces an explicit choice of isolation model — queued, per-worktree, or single-tree — rather than letting the run drift into the most dangerous one by default. |
| D-46 | `M4-S2`'s CP1 (`resolveAudience`, TDD) exposed a self-contradictory plan: an earlier draft listed `duplicate` as an `AudienceSkipReason` and asserted `actionable + skipped == totalMatched`. Writing the BR-CMP-002 unit test first (RED) failed `expected 2 to be 1` — BR-CMP-002's dedup is a *merge*, not a *skip*: a row that collapses into an already-seen person was never a second person to exclude, so counting it as `skipped` double-counts and the asserted identity cannot hold once `totalMatched` includes duplicate rows. | Removed `duplicate` from `AudienceSkipReason`; added a separate `deduplicated: number` field to `AudienceResolution`; the two real identities are `totalUnique + deduplicated == totalMatched` and `actionable + skipped == totalUnique`. Also fixed precedence: `deleted` → status → exclusion, tested explicitly so an unsubscribed-and-excluded recipient reports `status_unsubscribed` (a P0 compliance fact), never an exclusion reason that could mask it. Recorded in `M4-S2-AUDIENCE-PLAN.md` §3.2 rather than silently applied. |
| D-47 | `M4-S2`'s CP2 integration test for BR-CMP-002's duplicate-email case first tried two *active* recipients sharing an email and hit `idx_recipient_tenant_normalized_email_active`'s partial-unique constraint (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`) — proving that scenario is schema-impossible. Rewritten to the real case (a soft-deleted recipient's email reused by BR-GEN-006), this exposed a genuine ordering bug: the candidate query's `UNION ALL` has no guaranteed row order, and `resolveAudience()`'s first-seen-wins dedup could report the real, reachable active recipient as `deleted` if the deleted row happened to sort first, silently dropping a real recipient from the campaign. | Added `ORDER BY (deleted_at IS NOT NULL) ASC, recipient_id ASC` to `audience-candidates.repository.ts`'s query, documented in-code as a caller *contract* on `resolveAudience()` (anything producing candidates must order active-over-deleted for shared identities). Ran the integration test 3× to confirm determinism before trusting it. |
| D-48 | Seeding `M4-S2`'s visual-evidence fixtures through the real API (list/tag membership) hit a 400 on every call: `apps/api/src/segments/segments.controller.ts`'s `addListMembers`/`removeListMembers`/`updateList`/`addTagMembers`/`removeTagMembers`/`updateTag` all declared `@UsePipes(new ZodValidationPipe(bodySchema))` at the **method** level alongside a `@Param('id')` argument. NestJS runs method-level pipes against every resolved parameter, not just `@Body`, so the route id string was validated against the body's object schema and failed `(body): Invalid input: expected object, received string` — these six mutations have been unreachable over real HTTP since M2-S2 shipped them, masked because every existing test (`segments.test.ts`) called `SegmentsService` directly, never through the controller. | Moved each pipe onto its own `@Body()` parameter, matching `campaigns.controller.ts`'s already-correct pattern; added `segments-http.test.ts`'s new regression test exercising all six mutations end to end. Confirmed via curl before the fix (`400`, `expected object, received string`) and after (`201`/`200`/`204`). Full backend suite re-run clean (285/285) — the fix only changes pipe wiring, not `SegmentsService` behavior any existing test depended on. |
| D-49 | Following D-44's own suggested fix, `M4-S2`'s new visual-capture fixture helper (`seedAudienceFixture`) uses the app's absolute API base (`http://localhost:3000/api/v1`, matching `apps/web/src/api/campaigns.ts`'s default) instead of relative `/api/v1/...` fetches, and passed first try once written that way. D-44's root cause — `apps/web/vite.config.ts` has no `/api` dev-server proxy — is unchanged and still applies to every other relative-fetch fixture in `visual-capture.spec.ts`; not fixed here for the same reason D-44 gave (shared infrastructure, out of this node's scope). | No action beyond following the documented workaround. Recorded to close the loop on D-44 rather than leave it looking abandoned: the suggested fix works, is now used by a second author, and the underlying gap is still open for whichever node picks it up. |
| D-50 | Resuming this node after a context-window compaction, the previously-running `api` dev process (`node --watch apps/api/dist/main.js`, no start-time tsc-watch companion) was serving a **stale compiled `dist/`** — missing CP3's `previewCampaignAudience` route entirely (confirmed: source had 3 matches for `audience/preview`, compiled `campaigns.controller.js` had 0) — so the first browser verification pass 404'd against a route that had been in source and committed for two checkpoints already. A second confusion compounded it: `preview_start`'s tracked dev-server process runs from this session's git worktree, not the main-repo working tree every commit in this run actually lives on, so restarting through it produced `Cannot find module '...worktree.../apps/api/dist/main.js'` (never built there). | Rebuilt `apps/api` with a one-shot `tsc -p tsconfig.json` (confirmed the route now compiles into `dist/`), then started the api dev server via a plain background shell command `cd`'d into the main-repo path rather than through the worktree-scoped preview tool. Recorded as an operational gotcha for any future session resuming autonomous work on this run: after a compaction or a long gap, verify a live dev server's `dist/` actually contains the latest committed route (`grep` the compiled controller, not just `curl /health`) before trusting a 404 as a real finding. |
| D-76 | (renumbered from the originally-written D-51 — `M5-S1-sender-config`'s own reserved `D-*` block separately, correctly claimed D-51/D-52; caught while merging that node into `main`) `M4-S3`'s CP2 wiring of `computeCampaignVariableValidation` failed typecheck against `CampaignAudience`: `apps/api/src/database/entities/campaign.entity.ts`'s entity-level type never gained the `excludeRecipientIds` field that CP3 of `M4-S2` had already added to the DTO and OpenAPI schema — harmless at runtime (the column is `jsonb` and does not enforce the TypeScript shape) but a real, silent drift between the entity type and the actual persisted/serialized shape. | Added `excludeRecipientIds?: string[]` to the `CampaignAudience` entity interface, matching the DTO exactly; no migration needed since the column already accepts arbitrary JSON. Recorded as a caution for future nodes: a `jsonb` column's TypeScript entity type does not get checked against its DTO by anything mechanical, so a field added on one side can silently drift from the other until a later node's typecheck happens to touch it. |
| D-77 | (renumbered from the originally-written D-52, same collision as D-76 above) A background re-run of the 12 `M4-S3` visual-capture tests showed 10 of 12 failing on 30s `waitFor` timeouts (`templatePicker` desktop; all three `sendConfirm` states at desktop, tablet and mobile), while a prior run had already produced 12 apparently-good PNGs on disk. Rather than trust either the stale-but-present images or the failing re-run at face value, the environment was re-verified from scratch: Docker containers healthy, API `:3000` `/health` green, web dev server on `:5173` serving the current build (not a stale bundle, learning from D-50). Nothing was wrong with the environment; the exact `M4-S3` test group was re-run alone (not under whole-suite CPU contention) and came back a clean 12/12. | Individually inspected all 12 resulting images before accepting any of them as evidence (per AGENTS.md's standing discipline), confirming each showed the correct UI state at the correct viewport. Concluded the original 10-failure run was transient/environmental (most likely CPU contention from running alongside other verification work, in the same family as D-40's documented flake), not a product defect — recorded so a future session does not re-investigate the same false alarm from scratch. |
| D-51 | Migration `020_sender_config.sql` grants `sender_config`/`sending_policy` to `eow_app` and forces tenant RLS on both, but an owner-role (`postgres`) test connection bypasses RLS entirely by design — it cannot distinguish a correctly-scoped grant from a missing one. | `M5-S1`'s own migration/RLS tests, and this node's independent re-verification, connect exclusively as `eow_app` (never the owner role) so a forgotten `GRANT` or a missing `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` would actually fail the test instead of silently passing. |
| D-52 | `M5-S1` reuses the Nodemailer dependency already present in the workspace strictly as a **connection-probe** client (`transport.verify()`); no send path, queue, or worker is introduced by this slice. | `SmtpProviderAdapter.send()` deliberately throws `SENDING_DEFERRED_TO_M5_S3` rather than attempting a partial implementation — keeps this node's blast radius to configuration/verification only, consistent with `state.json`'s own slice boundary. |
| D-53 | `POST /sender-configs/:id/test-connection` resolves its probe target exclusively from the sender's own stored `host`/`port`/`username`/`secretRef`; the request body cannot supply or override a host or port. | Prevents the endpoint being used as an arbitrary outbound-connection oracle (SSRF-shaped risk) — a caller can only ever probe a configuration their own tenant already owns and stored. |
| D-54 | `SmtpProviderAdapter.testConnection()` bounds every probe to a 5-second `connectionTimeout`/`greetingTimeout`/`socketTimeout`, and `classifyError()` reduces the raw Nodemailer error to one of a fixed set of safe codes/classifications (`auth`/`config`/`transient`/`permanent`) before it ever reaches an HTTP response or an audit row. | Guarantees a slow/unreachable SMTP host cannot hang a request indefinitely, and guarantees raw provider error text (which can embed connection strings or credentials) never leaks through the API or audit surface — only the normalized classification does. |
| D-55 | The sender-config API response schema (`response()` in `sender-config.service.ts`) serializes only `maskSecretReference(row.secretRef)`, never a plaintext credential field; every credential-bearing input (`secret`) is write-only, accepted on create/update but never echoed back. | Confirmed genuinely enforced, not just schema-shaped: this node's live reproduction (Checkpoint 18) `JSON.stringify`'d the full create/get/list response bodies and confirmed the plaintext secret is absent while the masked `••••` form is present. |
| D-61 | `SenderConfigEntity.replyTo` (a `string \| null` TypeScript type) initially had no explicit TypeORM `@Column({ type: ... })`, which crashed `DataSource.initialize()` for the **entire application**, not just the sender-config module, the moment the entity was registered — TypeORM cannot infer a Postgres column type for a nullable string from the TS type alone. | Every entity column typed as `T \| null` or a string-literal union needs an explicit `type:` in its `@Column` options, matching the convention already established elsewhere in the codebase (D-66 is the same root cause applied to `provider`/`status`). Confirmed fixed this node: `replyTo`/`provider`/`status` all now carry explicit `type: 'text'`. |
| D-62 | `pnpm --filter @eow/api test` (`vitest run`) passing does **not** imply `pnpm --filter @eow/api build` (`tsc`) passing — vitest's esbuild transform does not enforce the same type checks `tsc` does, so a module can have 100% green unit tests while failing to compile. CP9 had only run `vitest run` before its self-report; a real `tsc` pass surfaced interface-mismatch errors in `SmtpProviderAdapter.send()`, `FakeProviderAdapter`'s call arity, and a `null`/`undefined` mismatch in `SenderSettingsScreen.tsx`. | Both `vitest run` and `tsc`/`pnpm build` must be run and confirmed independently — a green test suite alone is not evidence the code compiles. This node's own independent verification (Checkpoint 18) ran both separately rather than inferring one from the other. |
| D-63 | Nodemailer reports a refused SMTP connection as `error.code === 'ESOCKET'`, with the string `ECONNREFUSED` appearing only inside `error.message`, never as `error.code === 'ECONNREFUSED'` directly. Any adapter that classifies purely by `.code` misclassifies a connection-refused probe. | `SmtpProviderAdapter.classifyError()` inspects both the uppercased `code` and `message` text. Confirmed this node against a real, live `ECONNREFUSED`-via-`ESOCKET` error (a probe against a closed local port), correctly returning `'transient'` — not a code-only read, an actual reproduced probe. |
| D-64 | A `test-connection` call merely requiring the presence of an `Idempotency-Key` header is insufficient to prevent a duplicate probe/side-effect on retry — the existing `IdempotencyService` (backed by `idempotency_key`, already used elsewhere in the codebase) is the real deduplication boundary and must actually wrap the operation. | Confirmed this node via live reproduction: two `test-connection` calls with the identical key against a real closed port returned an identical body except `idempotencyReplayed: true` on the second — genuine replay through `IdempotencyService.run()`, not a header-presence check. |
| D-65 | Disabling a sender configuration must create a durable `notification`/`user_notification` row for the sender's owner (BR-CFG-007's "notification gửi owner" half) — a transient UI toast alone would not survive a page reload and would not satisfy the rule's literal acceptance text. | `SenderConfigService.disable()` inserts both rows directly inside the same transaction as the status flip. Confirmed this node via live reproduction: called `disable()` through the real HTTP endpoint, then queried `notification`/`user_notification` directly and found a real row, not just a code path that is never exercised. |
| D-66 | TypeORM cannot infer a Postgres column type for a nullable string or a TypeScript string-literal union (e.g. `SenderConfigStatus`) — the same root cause as D-61, but affecting `provider`/`status` rather than `replyTo`. | Every such sender-entity column now declares an explicit SQL `type:` (`provider`/`status` both `type: 'text'`). Confirmed present via direct entity read this node; app builds and boots clean. |
| D-67 | `/settings/policy` renders its finished form immediately, even while the underlying data fetch is still pending — so its `loading` and `empty` visual-capture states were byte-identical, and a "required state" cannot be treated as evidenced until the screen actually has a distinguishable one to capture. | A required visual state that renders identically to another required state is not really covered, regardless of what the capture spec's file list claims — this forced an actual loading-state component to be built before the state could be honestly marked `states_covered`. |
| D-68 | The policy screen's default-sender `<select>` lists only `verified` sender configs, so a "success" capture built from a freshly-created (`pending`) sender is visually indistinguishable from the screen's own `empty` state — the second instance of the D-42 failure mode (a green test having captured the wrong thing). | Visual success states must be driven through the real state transition (create → probe → verified) and must wait on the async data resolving, not on a heading or form that renders unconditionally regardless of fetch state. |
| D-69 | A Playwright run reporting success is not evidence by itself: the first M5-S1 visual-capture run failed 33/33 on a CORS-blocked login page, while the runner's own process exit status still read zero. | Only opening the actual captured images established what had truly been captured (a blocked login screen, not the target screens) — this is the visual-layer instance of D-38/D-42's "an artifact only a mechanical check opens is an artifact that drifts," applied here to a supposedly-green CI-style run rather than a hand-maintained YAML/CSV file. |
| D-70 | A **failing** Playwright run is not evidence either, symmetric to D-69 — a re-capture reporting 27/33 failures was caused entirely by a harness mistake (`E2E_BASE_URL` exported instead of the config's actually-read `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL`), not by any product defect. | Confirm the harness itself (which base URL/env var a config actually reads, which server is actually running on which port) before trusting either a green or a red run as evidence of product behavior. |
| D-71 | Two CSS rules can each look correct in isolation and still fight: `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`'s `@media(max-width:900px)` rule resets `.config-master-detail{height:auto}`, but a later, unconditional, higher-specificity rule added under a "Unified application frame" comment (`.settings-workspace>.config-master-detail{height:calc(100% - 64px)}`) wins at every width and silently cancels that reset. | Worth a dedicated specificity/cascade audit wherever this file layers a later "unified frame" pass over earlier per-component breakpoint rules — this is exactly the kind of defect a single-rule code read would miss, since both rules are independently well-formed. |
| D-72 | A fix that is provably correct in isolation (right selector, right specificity, right file, present in the built bundle) can still be a complete no-op if a second, unrelated ancestor constraint governs the same visual symptom at the same viewport — CP14's inner-grid fix for D-71 left a separate outer-frame breakpoint (1000px, not 900px/760px) still clipping tablet content. | Confirming a CSS fix requires checking that the *rendered pixels* actually changed, not just that the intended rule compiles and wins its own local cascade fight — reusing the pre-fix screenshot for a pixel diff is what caught this; a rule-level code read alone would have declared the fix complete. |
| D-73 | A passing full test-suite run plus a live, independently-authored (and immediately discarded) reproduction spec together satisfy "independently verified," even when the product's own authored test coverage has a known, disclosed gap (A1-A9 HTTP-level integration tests for sender-config do not exist as permanent suite members). | Provided the reproduction exercises the real service against real infra (not mocks) and is itself deleted rather than merged into the product suite — staying inside this protocol's "verify and report only, never fix Codex's product code" boundary while still closing the D-69/D-70 "self-report is not evidence" gap for backend behavior. |
| D-74 | Shared-artifact rule-ID citations must be checked against `traceability.csv`'s actual acceptance text, not assumed from context — a plausible-looking rule ID (`BR-CFG-006` "sounds like" a sender-config rule) can silently reference a different milestone's unrelated requirement. | Found and fixed one such mislabeling in Claude's own prior (Checkpoint 17) `screen-catalog.yaml` edit, which had borrowed `BR-CFG-006` (actually an M7 oversubscription/capacity-reservation rule) for the verified-default-sender policy constraint; corrected the prose and flagged to Codex that no exact rule_id currently exists for that constraint. |
| D-75 | A milestone node's own `state.json` `successConditions` text can itself name a future-milestone surface, the same failure mode DEC-027/044/045/046/049 found in individual `BR-*` rule acceptance text — `M5-S1`'s condition "policy... honoured by the worker" cannot be exercised until `M5-S3`'s send worker exists to read it. | Confirmed by grep: zero references to `sending_policy`/`SendingPolicy`/`defaultSenderConfigId` anywhere in `apps/worker`, consistent with `BR-CFG-004/005`'s own disclosed `partially_closed` send-path deferral. A node's own success-condition wording needs the same scope-honesty check as a traceability rule's acceptance text, not just the rules it lists. |
| D-78 | The D-71/D-72 CSS-cascade defect class (an outer `.settings-workspace`/`.workspace-module-frame` fixed-height rule at `@media(max-width:1000px)` clipping stacked tablet content, `globals.css` line ~175) recurs on `SenderSettingsScreen.tsx`'s own `.config-master-detail` markup: at 768×1024, `sender-configs-success-tablet-768x1024.png` shows the master list's 4th row bottom/status-dot clipped by the fixed-height frame; a milder floating/undersized detail-placeholder card appears in the empty and loading tablet states of the same screen. | Individually inspected all 33 M5-S1 visual-evidence images (13 sender-configs states/breakpoints, 3 create-overlay, 15 sending-policy) per the "never trust a passing test alone" discipline — the 393-test suite and Playwright capture run were both green; only per-image inspection surfaced this. The `sending-policy` screen's own 15 images (empty/error/loading/permission-denied/success × desktop/mobile/tablet) use a single-column form layout, not `.config-master-detail`, and show zero instances of either symptom, confirming the defect is scoped to `SenderSettingsScreen.tsx`'s master-detail markup specifically, not a blanket regression. |
| D-87 | `apps/worker/src/main.ts`'s five DAG-job stub cases (`validate`/`snapshot`/`send-batch`/`aggregate`/`notify`) reported fabricated success for every M5-S3 job: `validate` returned `{valid:true}` without reading anything, `snapshot` invented a UUID matching no row, `send-batch` reported `sent:n` having sent nothing and set progress to 100%. Same defect class as D-42/D-69. | Removed all five stubs at CP3, replaced by the real `campaign-send-scan` case driving the actual DAG (`validate.ts`/`partition.ts`/`send.ts`/`aggregate.ts`). |
| D-88 | `campaign_recipient.status` had no CHECK constraint since `001_initial.sql`, and M4-S4's freeze wrote `status:'queued'` for every frozen row including `eligibility='skipped'` ones — making BR-SEND-002's "counts sum to total_snapshot without double-counting skipped" arithmetically false by construction. | Migration 026 adds `campaign_recipient_status_known`/`campaign_recipient_skipped_status_agrees` CHECKs; the freeze correction (`campaign-snapshot.ts`) now writes `status: eligibility==='skipped' ? 'skipped' : 'pending'`. |
| D-89 | Four pre-existing test call sites (`campaign-snapshot-immutability.test.ts` x3, `recipients.test.ts` x1) wrote `campaign_recipient.status` values (`'sending'`/`'sent'`) outside BR-SEND-002's eight-value vocabulary — values borrowed from *campaign*-level states before the column had any vocabulary at all. Adding 026's CHECK would turn them red. | Fixture correction, not a weakened assertion: all four literals changed to `'submitted'`, which proves the same "column remains writable while frozen columns stay locked" claim identically. |
| D-90 | Neither `sender_config` nor `sending_policy` (migration 020) carried any rate-limit-shaping column, so BR-SEND-007 had nothing to obey in either direction: the worker didn't read a policy, and the policy had no fields to read. | Migration 026 adds `sender_config.rate_limit_per_minute`/`daily_send_limit` and `sending_policy.batch_size`/`max_attempts`/`tenant_rate_limit_per_minute`. |
| D-91 | `EnvSecretStore` (`apps/api/src/sender-config/secret-store.ts`) is a per-process in-memory `Map` with a `process.env` fallback — it cannot deliver a secret written by the API process to the separate worker process. A genuine architectural boundary, not a bug to silently paper over. | `validate.ts` resolves `secret_ref` from the worker's own `process.env` only; an unresolvable secret with non-empty `username` fails the execution with `failure_code='SENDER_SECRET_UNRESOLVED'` rather than falling back to an unauthenticated connection (DEC-100). |
| D-92 | `SmtpProviderAdapter.send()` threw `SENDING_DEFERRED_TO_M5_S3` and `getCampaignProgress` was still M4-S1's in-memory mock, returning fabricated counts *and a fabricated ETA* (`eta: now+8min`) for any campaign id, including nonexistent ones. | Both replaced: `sendClaimedBatch` submits for real; `getCampaignProgress` recomputes real counts via `GROUP BY status` and returns `eta: null` (BR-SEND-005/ETA is M6's, not this node's to fabricate), plus a real 404 for a nonexistent campaign. |
| D-93 | CP0 baseline: the full `docker compose` stack (`api`/`web`/`worker`/`scheduler` containers) competed for host CPU with vitest's own parallel test execution, producing `beforeAll` hook timeouts in 5 unrelated suites — not a product defect. | Stopped the 4 application containers, kept `postgres`/`redis`/`mailpit` running, and ran the API/web processes host-side for the remainder of this node (per the M5-S3 handoff's documented host-run pattern); `pnpm run check` re-ran clean (103 files/610 tests/0 skip/0 fail) before CP0 closed. |
| D-94 | CP1-CP3: recurring PostgreSQL `deadlock detected` errors, mostly at `ALTER TABLE ... DISABLE TRIGGER` cleanup steps, from concurrent test files under host contention — a distinct flake class from D-93's pure CPU-contention timeouts. | Isolated by re-running the affected suites standalone (always passed); disclosed as a known flake category rather than silently retried away. |
| D-95 | CP3: `run.ts`'s `currentExecutionId` fallback queried `campaign_execution`/`campaign_snapshot` without first setting `app.tenant_id`, so `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` silently returned zero rows instead of erroring — a real RLS bug, not a flake. | Wrapped the fallback query in `runInTenantContext`, matching every other tenant-scoped query in the codebase. |
| D-96 | CP3: the new system-wide `campaign-send-scan` was the first scan to claim rows on `campaign_recipient` itself (the M5-S2 dispatcher only ever touched `campaign`), so it could race an unrelated test file's `freezeCampaignSnapshot()` mid-test, producing FK violations during cleanup. | Added `message_attempt`/`campaign_execution` deletes before `campaign_snapshot` deletes to 7 pre-existing test cleanup routines. |
| D-97 | CP4: `sender.host` defaulted to the literal `'mailpit'` (the container-internal hostname), which does not resolve from host-run tests — matches the M5-S3 handoff's documented host-run-vs-docker gotcha. | Test `beforeAll` hooks set `process.env.SMTP_HOST='127.0.0.1'`/`SMTP_PORT='1025'` explicitly. |
| D-98 | CP4: `ALTER TABLE ... DISABLE/ENABLE TRIGGER` issued via separate `pool.query()` calls is not connection-scoped — each call can round-robin to a different physical connection from the pool, so the pre-existing `pg_advisory_xact_lock` guard did nothing useful. Adding 3 more worker test files with the same copied pattern made this latent bug manifest as a spurious `"Published template versions are immutable"` error mid-cleanup. | Extracted `apps/worker/src/test-cleanup-helpers.ts`'s `purgeCampaignSendFixtures()`, running the whole disable/delete/enable sequence on one checked-out client inside one real transaction; updated all 5 affected worker test files to use it. |

| D-100 | CP5: the A13/A14 rate-limit test fixtures never set `campaign_execution.sender_config_id` (only `campaign_snapshot.sender_json`), so the rate/daily-limit code path was silently never exercised — caught because RED unexpectedly came back GREEN (4 submitted instead of the expected 2). | Fixed the shared `fixtureExecution` test helper to also set `sender_config_id`; re-confirmed genuine RED before the real fix, then GREEN after. |
| D-101 | ARCH-TEST-HYGIENE's hardcoded-connection-string fitness function flagged the deliberately-unreachable Redis URL literal (`'redis://127.0.0.1:1'`) used by a DEC-103 fail-closed test. | Added a justified entry to `STATIC_URL_ALLOWED` in `packages/architecture-tests/src/test-hygiene.test.ts`, matching the pre-existing `boot.test.ts` precedent. |
| D-102 | CP6: `partitionBatch`'s `'drained'` outcome (all sendable rows already claimed) short-circuited `runOneCampaign` to `null` before send/aggregate ever ran — so any campaign whose partition pass drained on a given scan tick would never be revisited, stuck in `'sending'` forever. A real bug, not a hypothetical: it breaks retries and multi-batch completion. | Both `'partitioned'` and `'drained'` now fall through to send+aggregate; only `'not_claimable'` short-circuits. |
| D-103 | CP6: `rbac-matrix.test.ts`'s `/progress` probe asserted `200` for a random UUID naming no real campaign — it only ever passed because the old fabricated mock (D-92) never checked existence. | D-104 | CP8: the sending/completed/partial_failed/failed banners rendered the correct static status label immediately, but the `.send-summary` counts tiles were completely absent from the captured e2e screenshots because `getCampaignProgress`'s async fetch had not resolved yet at shutter time — the Playwright wait only covered the static label text, not the data. Caught specifically because every image was individually inspected per D-78's standing discipline, rather than trusting the green 15/15 Playwright run. | Added `await page.locator('.frozen-banner .send-summary').waitFor()` before each of the 4 affected screenshots in `visual-capture.spec.ts`; re-ran all 15 tests and re-inspected every image before accepting them as evidence. |
| D-105 | M5-S4 (pre-registered at planning, fixed at CP5): `getCampaignProgress`'s `sent: counts.submitted` decreases the instant a delivery webhook moves a row from `submitted` to `delivered`/`bounced` — a webhook could never fire before M5-S4, so M5-S3 could not have observed this. Violates AGENTS.md §4's "progress counters never decrease" the moment CP4's first webhook lands. | `sent`/`delivered`/`failed` recomputed as monotonic rollups over `counts.{submitted,delivered,bounced,failed}` rather than raw passthroughs; RED-first `campaign-send-http.test.ts` case drives submit→deliver→bounce and confirms `sent` no longer regresses. `counts` itself is untouched. |
| D-106 | M5-S4 (pre-registered at planning, fixed at CP4): `SmtpProviderAdapter.parseWebhookEvent` spread the entire caller-controlled payload into its return value with an `as` cast — harmless only because nothing called it before this node wired it to a public route. | Replaced with a parser that reads exactly four named fields and ignores every other key, including a payload shaped like a cross-tenant write attempt; `ProviderWebhookEvent` widened accordingly; RED-first `smtp-provider.adapter.test.ts`. |
| D-107 | M5-S4 (pre-registered at planning, fixed at CP1/CP4): `message_attempt.provider_message_id`, the only join key from a webhook callback back to a recipient, was neither indexed nor uniquely constrainable — the deterministic Message-ID (DEC-104) legitimately repeats across a crash-retry resubmission for the *same* recipient. | `idx_message_attempt_provider_message_id` added (027); `resolve_provider_message()` selects `DISTINCT`, so a same-recipient duplicate collapses to one row while a genuinely ambiguous mapping across two different recipients (D-107's own integration test) correctly returns two and is treated as unmatched (DEC-111). |
| D-108 | M5-S4 CP2: `suppressRecipient()` was specified as a standalone function in M5-S3's own plan (DEC-105 promised "M5-S4 only has to add a caller, not a mechanism"), but what shipped was an inline four-statement tail inside `send.ts`'s `recordFailure`, with no second caller reachable. A new RED test proved the gap concretely: a second hard bounce against an already-suppressed recipient bumped `recipient.version` again and wrote a second `recipient.suppressed` audit row. | Extracted `apps/worker/src/campaign-send/suppression.ts`; added `AND suppressed_at IS NULL` so a repeat suppression is a no-op (first cause wins); transliterated for `apps/api` (cross-app import is structurally impossible, DEC-107) with `ARCH-SUPPRESSION-PARITY` holding the two in step. |
| D-109 | M5-S4 CP3 (found during implementation, not pre-registered): the plan's original raw-body design was route-scoped `express.raw()` middleware. Verified against `@nestjs/platform-express` source before implementing: `main.ts`'s global `app.useBodyParser('json', …)` drains the request stream for any `application/json` request before a route-scoped parser added later in the bootstrap sequence ever runs — a second parser would silently read an already-empty buffer in production, not error. A silent-wrong-data defect, not a loud one. | Replaced with NestJS's built-in `NestApplicationOptions.rawBody` (a `verify` callback on the one existing JSON parser), confirmed identical between `NestFactory.create()` and `TestingModule.createNestApplication()` against both sources — satisfies the original test/prod-parity goal (DEC-112) more literally than module-scoped middleware did. |
| D-110 | M5-S4 CP4 (found while writing the A7 integration test, not pre-registered): the decision table as first written checked transition legality before staleness. Because `delivered`/`bounced` each have exactly one inbound edge and zero outbound edges, the row leaves `submitted` permanently the instant the first such event applies — so a *second* event of the same class, checked legality-first, always fails `isLegalMessageTransition` regardless of its own timestamp, making `ignored_out_of_order` structurally unreachable by any two sequential requests. No test could be written to prove A7 under that order. | Staleness now checked first: an event no newer than the row's own `delivery_state_at` is reported `ignored_out_of_order` whether or not it would also have been an illegal transition; only a genuinely newer event goes on to the legality check. `apply-decision.ts`/`apply-decision.test.ts` and this plan's §3.4b corrected together, before either was committed. |
| D-111 | M5-S4 CP7 (found deploying the feature, not pre-registered): `compose.yaml`'s `${EOW_PROVIDER_WEBHOOK_SECRET:-}` expands to an empty string, not an absent key, when unset in `.env`. An empty string is not `undefined`, so `env.ts`'s plain `z.string().min(32).optional()` rejected it and the `api` container crash-looped on every boot — in exactly the "no provider callbacks configured" deployment this variable's own default is meant to support. Reproduced live via `docker compose up -d --build`. | `PROVIDER_WEBHOOK_SECRET`'s Zod schema preprocesses an empty string to `undefined` before validation; RED-first `env.test.ts` cases; rebuilt and confirmed the container healthy, `scripts/smoke_deploy.py` green, and a live `curl` against the running route returning the expected 503. |
| D-112 | M5-GATE (found evaluating BR-CFG-002, `not_started` since M5-S1 CP18 explicitly deferred it): `sendCampaign` froze the snapshot and moved the campaign straight to `queued` without consulting `buildScheduleValidationReport` or any sender check at all — `POST /campaigns/{id}/send` accepted 202 for a campaign whose sender was `pending`, `failed` or `disabled`. | D-113 | M5-GATE (found evaluating BR-CFG-002): `apps/worker/src/campaign-send/validate.ts` checked only `sender.status === 'disabled'`, so a `pending` or `failed` sender passed time-of-use validation and the batch proceeded to submit. Two `A17` assertion failures observed in the same runs were a downstream symptom of this crash (a crashed `A15`/`A16` run left an execution un-reconciled for `A17`'s later cross-tenant scan to pick up out of order), not separate bugs; both resolved once the root cause did. | D-123 | M6-S3 CP1 (found verifying BR-SEND-009's 10-second bound against the scheduler's actual tick, not pre-registered): `apps/scheduler/src/main.ts` defaults `SCHEDULER_TICK_MS` to 60,000ms. Setting `campaign.status='paused'` correctly removes a campaign from the next scan pass, but a batch already claimed inside `sendClaimedBatch` would otherwise keep submitting for up to a full `batchSize` (default 100) messages — several minutes at typical throughput — before the next tick even runs, missing BR-SEND-009's 10-second bound by more than an order of magnitude. | Pause is enforced inside `sendClaimedBatch`'s own per-row loop via a bounded re-check (`PAUSE_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS = 2000`), not by removing the campaign from the scan alone (CP5, ADR-027). |
| D-124 | M6-S3 CP5 (found running the full `apps/worker` suite after implementing pause enforcement, not hypothetical): a naive `status !== 'sending'` pause check made `send.integration.test.ts`'s own A7 600-recipient throttle test fail — that fixture deliberately parks `campaign.status` at a value outside `queued_campaign_executions()`'s `IN`-list to hide from the live docker-compose worker's cross-tenant scan (D-118's mechanism), and had used `'paused'` for that purpose since M6-S1, back when `'paused'` carried no enforcement meaning at all. | D-125 | M6-S3 CP7 (found implementing export creation, not pre-registered): `apps/api/src/campaigns/exports.service.ts`'s own new `UPDATE export_job ... RETURNING` returned `500` — traced to a genuine, previously undetected defect in TypeORM's `manager.query()`: for `UPDATE`/`DELETE` statements it returns `[rows, affectedCount]` (a nested 2-element array), unlike `SELECT`/`INSERT`, which return bare `rows`. Confirmed empirically against the running database. Two already-closed instances of the same mistake were found by grepping every `RETURNING` clause in `apps/api/src`: `progress-snapshot.ts`'s `writeProgressSnapshot` (`const [row] = ... as Array<{progress_seq}>` — `row` was actually the rows array, so `Number(row.progress_seq)` was `NaN` on every call, serialising to `null` on the wire — silently defeating D-116/DEC-122's entire monotonic dedupe-key mechanism for `campaign.progress` events, since M6-S1 shipped it) and `campaigns.service.ts`'s `cancelCampaignSend` (`cancelled.length` was always `2` regardless of the real cancelled-row count — masked in the one existing test because its fixture happened to have exactly 2 cancellable rows). Two more confirmed instances (`segments.service.ts`'s `removeList`/`removeTag`, where the same mistake makes their `NotFoundException` branch permanently dead code, and `notifications.service.ts`'s `purgeExpired`, whose wrong count is currently discarded by both callers) are out of this node's rule ownership (BR-SEG-*, BR-NOT-*) and were disclosed as a spawned follow-up task rather than fixed here. | `exports.service.ts` fixed at introduction (never shipped broken). `progress-snapshot.ts` and `campaigns.service.ts` fixed with RED-first regression tests: a new `progress-snapshot-db.test.ts` (the first test file ever written directly against this function, asserting the real numeric return value and the JSON-serialised wire payload, not a re-read from a fresh SELECT the way every prior indirect test did) and two new cases in `campaign-send-http.test.ts` using cancelled counts of 1 and 0 specifically to defeat the "always 2" masking a plain re-run of the existing fixture could not have caught. |

Every Playwright assertion still passed, because `getByRole('dialog', {name: ...})` matches on the accessible-name/role of the DOM node regardless of whether it is visually clipped to zero-effective-area by an ancestor's `overflow:hidden` — the exact failure mode D-78/D-104 warned about, now reconfirmed a third time. Re-ran the two affected Playwright tests and re-inspected all 4 resulting screenshots (filter dialog, resend confirm, resend success) — all now render as proper centered modal overlays with the dimmed backdrop. `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| D-150 | M7-S2 CP4: CSV export cells beginning `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` were emitted unescaped — spreadsheet formula injection, the spreadsheet-equivalent of stored XSS and part of `TC-SEC-014`'s own scope. | Neutralised at the export render boundary with a regression test. |
| D-167 | Review session, merge 2 (found by running M7-S2's own new rule against the merged tree): `ARCH-CROSS-TENANT` failed naming `PUT /sending-quota` and `GET /sending-quota/usage` uncovered. M7-S1 wrote a cross-tenant negative for `GET /sending-quota` only, and the rule that would have caught the other two did not exist on M7-S1's branch — neither node could have seen this alone. | Fixed by **adding** the missing coverage to `apps/api/test/integration/sending-quota-http.test.ts`, never by weakening the rule (AGENTS.md §5). Two secondary defects surfaced while writing it: that file's `login()` helper is an *operator* while `PUT /sending-quota` requires `SETTINGS_MANAGE` (admin), and its teardown deleted `app_user` before `sending_policy`, whose `updated_by` references it. Both fixed. This is the precise class of gap that sequential merge-and-verify exists to find, and the first hard evidence in this run that the one-at-a-time rule earns its cost. |
| D-166 | Review session, merge 1 (found establishing the pre-merge baseline): `apps/worker/src/campaign-send/run.integration.test.ts`'s "re-running the scan over an already-sending campaign is a safe no-op" fails under full-suite contention on the review machine — `expected undefined to be 'completed_empty'` — on **clean `main` before any M7 merge**, and passes alone twice immediately afterward. `apps/api`'s `boot.test.ts` shows the same class of failure (D-40) when a concurrent 1040-file checkout runs beside it. | Pre-existing flake, not introduced by any M7 node; recorded so it is not attributed to one at M7-GATE. Distinct from D-142, which is the execution host's flake in a different file. Two hosts, two different contention-sensitive integration tests — the suite has a real parallel-isolation weakness worth its own hardening task. |
---

## 20. Decision Log

| ID | Decision | Rationale | Alternative rejected |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DEC-001 | Mode is GRAPH with bounded node LOOPs | `.agents/orchestration-policy.yaml` → `default_after_approval`; multi-domain dependencies and real parallelism exist | LOOP only — would serialise 22 independent slices |
| DEC-002 | Raw SQL migrations remain the mechanism; TypeORM is used for entity mapping only | ADR-024 makes `database/migrate.sh` the deployment contract and it is newer than ADR-007; DEPLOY-005 depends on file checksums | TypeORM migrations — would break the checksum guard and ADR-024 |
| DEC-003 | Introduce real routes in `apps/web` | `BR-NOT-010`, `BR-HIS-003`, `BR-AUTH-004` are unsatisfiable with in-memory view state. Presentation is unchanged, so this is not a redesign. | Preserve single-page state navigation — would make three accepted rules unimplementable |
| DEC-004 | Socket.IO per ADR-009 + `asyncapi.yaml`; no SSE endpoint | Accepted ADR and the realtime contract outrank OD-08 (an *open* decision) and `api-baseline.json` (reviewed intent). `GET /campaigns/{id}/events` is not implemented. | SSE + polling — would contradict an Accepted ADR and the AsyncAPI contract |
| DEC-005 | Ship the HTML/code editor from the handoff; do not adopt GrapesJS in MVP | ADR-019 is **Proposed**, not Accepted, and requires a spike first. The handoff's editor is the approved presentation. | Adopt GrapesJS now — would act on a non-accepted ADR and redesign an approved screen |
| DEC-053 | Omit the handoff `draftActions` “Xuất file HTML” button in M4-S1. | No campaign-draft export endpoint or owning M4 business rule exists; omitting it avoids UI theatre and invented scope while retaining real duplicate, rename and delete actions. | Add a toast/placeholder or serialize incidental HTML — rejected because neither is backed by a contract or rule. |
| DEC-055 | Run `M4-S2`, `M5-S1` and `M6-S2` under an explicit written protocol (`PARALLEL-EXECUTION-PROTOCOL.md`): reserved migration numbers and `DEC-*`/`D-*` blocks, disjoint OpenAPI path prefixes edited once and committed immediately, a named single owner per contended source file, an `inbox/<node-id>.md` checkpoint record replacing direct writes to the four shared run artifacts, and a mandatory **visual handoff** where Codex writes capture code but never runs, inspects or judges an image. | AGENTS.md §2 states git gives recovery and visibility but not mutual exclusion; on one shared working tree three concurrent nodes silently overwrite each other, and the four run artifacts are both the most-written and the least-verifiable files (D-42 corrupted `traceability.csv` invisibly with only *one* node active). The visual split is not a preference: Codex cannot inspect images, and M4-S1 shipped three green screenshot tests that had all captured a loading spinner (D-42) — an agent that cannot look at output cannot own evidence that only looking can validate. | Let all three write the shared artifacts directly and rely on git — rejected: on one tree there is no merge, only last-writer-wins. Serialise everything through one agent — rejected: that is just sequential execution, which the protocol offers as option (A) but the human asked for parallelism. Skip visual evidence for these nodes — rejected: `visual-acceptance.md` and Definition of Done both require it, and M4-S1 proved unverified captures actively mislead. |
| DEC-054 | Do not render the handoff `draftSaved` modal for autosave. | M4-S1 has debounced autosave and no explicit save action; the inline editor and shell save-state indicators are the real BR-CMP-001 feedback. | Fire the modal on every autosave — rejected as noisy and semantically wrong; trigger it from a future explicit save/exit action when that action exists. |
| DEC-056 | `M4-S2` adds no migration; audience resolution is a query over existing `recipient`/`recipient_list_member`/`recipient_tag` (all from `006_recipient_extensions.sql`/`010_recipient_lists_tags.sql`). Reserved migration `019` stays unused unless a real index need is measured with `EXPLAIN` against a realistic tenant. | A `campaign_audience` cache table that can disagree with the live query is exactly the class of bug M4-S4's snapshot exists to make impossible — inventing one before the authoritative snapshot exists would create two sources of truth. | Cache the resolved audience in a new table for preview speed — rejected: nothing in this slice's acceptance criteria needs it, and it front-runs M4-S4's actual snapshot design. |
| DEC-057 | BR-CMP-003 names five exclusion causes (unsubscribed, paused, hard-bounced, deleted, **suppressed**); `M4-S2` implements the first four and declares `suppressed` unimplementable in this slice rather than mapping it onto `bounced` or inventing a suppression list. `AudienceSkipReason` has no `suppressed` member. | No suppression list exists anywhere in the schema, `catalog/ba-rules.json`, or any node's owned scope — reusing `bounced` would conflate two different compliance facts (a hard bounce vs. an operator/provider suppression decision), and a rule's fifth clause should not quietly evaporate. Matches the DEC-044/045/046/049 precedent of reallocating a sub-clause explicitly rather than silently dropping it. | Treat `bounced` as the suppression mechanism for MVP — rejected: conflates distinct causes and would need unwinding once a real suppression list exists. Owner for `suppressed`: whichever node first introduces a suppression/unsubscribe-list concept (candidate: M7-S1 quota/compliance infrastructure, or a dedicated M5 provider-webhook node if bounce/complaint handling grows one first) — not yet assigned to a specific node. |
| DEC-058 | BR-CMP-009's over-limit response is a **configured static ceiling** (`CAMPAIGN_AUDIENCE_LIMIT` env var, default 100,000) returning 422 with `limit`/`current`/`requested`; `M4-S2` does not build 429 or any quota-consumption ledger. | No quota ledger exists until M7-S1 (BR-CFG-006 and reservation/release machinery). 429 belongs with rate-limited quota consumption, which this slice does not own; shipping a 429 path with no real quota behind it would be evidence theatre. | Build a minimal quota ledger now so both 422 and 429 are real — rejected: front-runs M7-S1's actual design and scope, for a code path (429) no acceptance criterion in this slice exercises. |
| DEC-059 | `recipientPicker`'s three scope decisions, made porting the handoff's `action-overlays.tsx` L106-109 DOM: (1) omit the handoff's third "Import Excel" tab — M2-S4 already owns turning a spreadsheet into real recipients, and `resolveAudience()` only operates over existing `recipient` rows; (2) add a "Bao gồm/Loại trừ" (Include/Exclude) toggle the handoff does not have at all, reusing its own two-button `.tag-logic` vocabulary — BR-SEG-009 is an owned rule requiring exclusion to be expressible, and the handoff has zero exclusion affordance; (3) omit the handoff's `recipient-variable-audit` missing-template-data warning panel — that is BR-TPL-*/M4-S3's variable policy, not this node's audience resolution. | Each decision keeps the overlay's real capability aligned with what this slice actually owns: building a second import path duplicates M2-S4, refusing to add exclusion UI would leave an owned P1 rule (BR-SEG-009) without a way to trigger it, and porting the variable-audit panel would fake a capability (missing-variable detection) this node does not implement. | Port the handoff's Excel tab as a thin re-export of M2-S4's import flow — rejected: no acceptance criterion needs it and it adds a second entry point for the same action. Skip the Include/Exclude toggle and require exclusions via a future overlay — rejected: BR-SEG-009 is owned by this node now, not deferred. |
| DEC-060 | Removed the dead, arch_contracts-era `validateTemplateAudience` OpenAPI stub (template-scoped, never had a real caller, wrong resource shape for what M4-S3 needed) and replaced it with two campaign-scoped operations, `validateCampaignAudience` and `acceptCampaignAudienceWaiver`. | `M4-S3`'s actual behaviour validates an audience against a *campaign's* selected template and recipients, not a template in isolation; keeping the old stub alongside the new real operations would leave a never-implemented, never-called path in the contract indefinitely. | Implement behind the old template-scoped stub's shape instead — rejected: the stub's resource path and request shape do not match what a campaign-scoped waiver/validation flow actually needs, and bending the new design to fit a dead stub would be shape-driven-by-accident rather than by the rule. |
| DEC-075 | (renumbered from the originally-written DEC-061 — colliding with `M5-S1-sender-config`'s own reserved `DEC-061..DEC-065` block, caught while merging that node into `main`) BR-TPL-008 is closed on the validation-blocks-send half for real (a recipient missing a required template variable blocks send unless waived); `unsubscribeUrlFor` generates a real, unique, per-recipient URL from `WEB_ORIGIN` today, but it is deliberately left **unsigned** — no public unsubscribe-redemption endpoint exists yet anywhere in the 118-rule catalog. | Signing (and verifying) an unsubscribe token is only meaningful once something actually consumes it; building a signature scheme for an endpoint that does not exist would be speculative security theatre with no acceptance criterion to validate it against. | Sign the URL now against a future endpoint's assumed contract — rejected: the future endpoint's real shape is unknown, and an unverified signing scheme invented ahead of its consumer is more likely to need rework than to be reused as-is. Leave `unsubscribeUrlFor` unimplemented entirely — rejected: recipients still need a real, working URL in the rendered email today, even before redemption exists. |

| DEC-006 | MVP provider is the SMTP adapter against Mailpit | OD-05 is open; ADR-014's adapter boundary keeps the choice reversible; `compose.yaml` already provides Mailpit | Block on provider selection — would stall M5 on a decision that is not a STOP gate |

Mirrors the existing `EOW_MAILPIT_BIND` pattern already in `compose.yaml`. | Run tests inside a container joined to the Compose network instead — rejected as materially more complex on this Windows/Docker-Desktop host (native module cross-compilation for the containerized runner) for no correctness benefit over a loopback bind |
| DEC-017 | Fixed the specific YAML-breaking lines found in `ui-inventory.yaml` (D-21) but did not do a full pass hardening every special character in that file | The file is planning documentation, not consumed by running code; a full pass is disproportionate to `arch_contracts`' scope. `screen-catalog.yaml` was hand-written from already-known data instead of programmatically extracted from the broken fields. | Fully re-write `ui-inventory.yaml` for strict YAML compliance now — rejected as scope creep beyond the approved implementation work |
| DEC-018 | Session model is a single rotating opaque token (`user_session.refresh_token_hash` doubles as the session credential's hash), not a separate short-lived JWT access token + long-lived refresh token pair | `002_identity_and_audit.sql` (delivered by `arch_contracts`) only defines one token table with one hash column; the OpenAPI `sessionCookie` security scheme is a single named cookie (`eow_session`). `POST /auth/refresh` still satisfies BR-AUTH-002's literal acceptance test ("refresh token cũ không dùng lại được sau rotation") via revoke-and-reissue with `rotated_from` linkage. | Add a second `access_token`/JWT layer on top — would require a schema change beyond what 002 provides and duplicate session state that 002's single table already models; deferred as unnecessary complexity unless a future rule requires it |
| DEC-019 | Lockout (BR-AUTH-005) is keyed by email only, not per-tenant, and an unknown email is still recorded (with `tenant_id = NULL`, per `003_password_reset.sql`'s relaxed column) | `LoginRequest` carries no tenant selector — the API cannot know which tenant an email belongs to before resolving it, and an attacker probing an unregistered email must still be throttled the same as one probing a real account (BR-AUTH-001's no-enumeration principle applied to lockout, not just the error message) | Key lockout by `(tenantId, email)` — rejected: unknown-email attempts would then need either a fabricated tenant (data-integrity violation) or no throttling at all (enumeration + brute-force loophole) |
| DEC-020 | The full 39-overlay system (`action-overlays.tsx`) and command palette are not ported in this node; their trigger buttons render with the handoff's DOM but show a "coming soon" toast | Overlays are catalogued across M2-M7 in `ui-inventory.yaml`, not owned by M1-S1. | DEC-021 | `Secure` cookie attribute is keyed off `WEB_ORIGIN`'s scheme (`https://`), not `NODE_ENV` | See D-23 — `NODE_ENV=production` is set by `compose.yaml` even for this project's intentionally-plain-HTTP one-command deployment baseline; a `NODE_ENV`-keyed `Secure` flag would silently break every login once actually deployed via `docker compose up` | Key off `NODE_ENV` (the "obvious" choice) — rejected after cross-referencing `docs/deployment/quick-deploy.md`'s explicit plain-HTTP baseline and "TLS at the edge" deferral |
| DEC-022 | `apps/api/package.json`'s `dev` script (`tsx watch src/main.ts`) is left unchanged despite D-22 (it silently breaks constructor DI for any endpoint with a real dependency) | A correct cross-platform fix changes how *every* future controller is developed/debugged and is not specific to sign-in; the actual deployment path (`tsc` build + `node dist/main.js`, what `Dockerfile`'s `api`/`worker`/`scheduler` targets run) is unaffected and is what this node used for its own manual/E2E verification | Silently patch `tsx watch` behaviour or swap the dev runner now — rejected as scope creep with monorepo-wide blast radius; flagged in D-22 for a dedicated future node instead |
| DEC-023 | A new forward migration `003_password_reset.sql` relaxes `login_attempt.tenant_id` (created by `002`, delivered by `arch_contracts`) from `NOT NULL` to nullable, rather than adding a workaround table | `002` is not a published/Accepted-ADR-frozen migration in the same sense as `001` — it was delivered earlier in *this same run* and is still evolving forward within the run's own scope; `login_attempt.tenant_id` being `NOT NULL` was a real gap once BR-AUTH-005's "throttle by email/IP even for an unknown account" requirement was implemented (DEC-019). Forward-only (`003` alters what `002` created), `001` and `002`'s own files are untouched. | Fabricate a placeholder/system tenant for unknown-email attempts — rejected as a data-integrity smell (a `login_attempt` row implying tenant membership that never existed) |
| DEC-024 | `role` and `permission` are global reference catalogues (no `tenant_id`); only `user_role` (the assignment of a role to a user) is tenant-owned | BR-AUTH-003 says the *system* has at minimum Admin/Operator/Viewer — a fixed capability model, not a per-tenant configurable one. AGENTS.md's "every tenant-owned row carries `tenant_id`" invariant applies to rows describing tenant-owned *facts* (who has what role), not to the shared definition of what a role or permission *is*. | Make `role`/`permission` per-tenant too — rejected as unnecessary complexity with no BA rule requiring tenant-customisable roles, and it would make the seeded catalogue non-deterministic across tenants for zero acceptance-criteria benefit |
| DEC-025 | `rbac.role_assigned` audit rows for the *existing*-user backfill are written directly by the `004_rbac.sql` migration's own `INSERT ... SELECT` (actor_id `NULL`, trace_id `'migration:004_rbac'`), not by a new role-assignment API endpoint | No BA rule or this node's numbered steps require a role-assignment endpoint; inventing one would be scope creep into a feature no milestone currently owns. | DEC-026 | `PermissionGuard` denies (with an audit row) any route that declares **neither** `@Public()` **nor** `@RequirePermission()`, rather than treating an undeclared route as implicitly public or implicitly permitted | BR-AUTH-004's literal text is "API kiểm quyền phía server" (server-side permission checking) as the default posture, not an opt-in one; deny-by-default is explicitly named in this node's success conditions. An undeclared route is far more likely to be an oversight than an intentional public endpoint. | Default undeclared routes to "authenticated but no extra permission needed" — rejected: this would silently under-protect any future controller a developer forgets to annotate, exactly the failure mode BR-AUTH-004 exists to prevent |
| DEC-027 | Reallocated `BR-GEN-003` (timezone/UTC display) from M1 to M5 (`M5-S2`), and `BR-GEN-005` (Idempotency-Key on send/import/bulk-update) from M1 to M2 (`M2-S4`), in `traceability-plan.yaml` and `traceability.csv`. `BR-SEC-008` (API versioning) stays `not_started` in M1, disclosed as a known gap for `M1-GATE`, without reallocation. | Both `BR-GEN-003`/`BR-GEN-005`'s acceptance criteria name concrete surfaces (schedule; send/import/bulk-update jobs) that do not exist until `M5-S2`/`M2-S4` — attempting to close them in `M1-S3` ("Audit and errors") would mean either inventing out-of-scope M2/M5 endpoints just to manufacture evidence, or a synthetic test with no real behaviour behind it. Both are P0, so `traceability-plan.yaml`'s own `waived` status was not an option either (`status_values` doc: "P0 rules may not be waived"). Left un-reallocated, they would have permanently blocked `M1-GATE`'s "all M1 rules closed" condition without a path to ever genuinely satisfy it from inside M1 — a real deadlock, since `M1-GATE` gates M2's and (indirectly, via `M4-GATE`) M5's own start. `BR-SEC-008` has no such deadlock (nothing about closing it requires a future milestone's *feature* to exist, only undone process/test work), so it was left in place rather than moved on no clearer a home than "wherever a future breaking-contract-change event actually happens". | Force a synthetic closure now (e.g. a fake `timezone_name` field with no real caller, or an idempotency middleware guarding an endpoint invented solely to close the rule) — rejected as evidence-less box-checking, contradicting this run's evidence discipline everywhere else. Leave the rule allocation as originally planned and let `M1-GATE` simply fail/block on it forever — rejected: that is not a STOP gate (no missing authority, secret, or business ambiguity), it is a fixable planning-arithmetic defect, and `AGENTS.md` §5's routine-repair carve-out (`"routine code choices ... are not reasons to ask the human again"`) applies the same way it did to the `migrate.sh` (D-16) and `tsx` decorator-metadata (D-20/D-22) bugs found earlier in this run. |
| DEC-028 | `BR-SEC-008` (API versioning, P2) is `waived` in M1, not closed and not reallocated. Compensating control: `scripts/openapi-compat-check.mjs` is wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` as a required check and is now proven by a real, runnable test (`scripts/openapi-compat-check.test.mjs`, `node --test`, 3/3 passing) that it (a) blocks a deliberately-introduced breaking change — a removed `401` response and a removed required `Problem.status` — with exit 1 naming both breaks, (b) passes a genuinely additive change, and (c) treats a first-ever spec (no prior baseline) as compatible. No breaking change has been made or is currently planned anywhere in this run. | `M1-GATE` investigated the rule's *full* acceptance text — "Breaking change tạo version mới; OpenAPI và error schema được publish cùng release" (a breaking change creates a new version; OpenAPI and the error schema are published with that release) — and found it names a version-creation mechanism (e.g. an `/api/v2` prefix or content negotiation) that does not exist anywhere in this codebase and that no current milestone's scope requires, since every contract change made so far (`arch_contracts`, `M1-S1`, `M1-S2`) has been additive by construction. Claiming "closed" on the strength of the backward-compatibility gate alone would overclaim: that gate *prevents* a breaking change from landing silently, it does not *version* one. `BR-SEC-008` is P2, so `waived` is policy-available (`traceability-plan.yaml`'s `waiver_rule`: "P0 rules may not be waived" — P1/P2 may). This decision is itself the required Decision Log entry per that rule. | Build a minimal versioning mechanism now solely to close the rule (e.g. an unused `/api/v2` alias with no behavioural difference) — rejected as evidence-less box-checking, the same failure mode DEC-027 rejected for `BR-GEN-003`/`BR-GEN-005`. Leave it silently `not_started` — rejected: that is exactly the "disclosed known gap" the node's own success conditions forbid leaving unresolved. |

Compensating control: soft-delete behaviour is verified for real as each entity ships — `M2-S1-recipients`'s own success conditions already require "soft delete and unsubscribed status behave per rule" (`state.json`), and the same pattern is expected of `M2-S2-lists-tags`, `M3-S1-templates` and `M4-S4-snapshot`'s campaign handling. `documentation_final` must confirm the pattern actually holds for all five entity types (with real `code_paths`/`test_files`) before `BR-GEN-006` can be flipped from `waived` to `closed`. | `BR-GEN-006`'s acceptance criteria name five entity types, none of which exist in M1 (M1 has only `tenant`/`app_user`/session/audit/RBAC tables) — the same "acceptance criteria names a surface that doesn't exist yet" shape as `BR-GEN-003`/`BR-GEN-005` (DEC-027). Unlike those two, it spans *multiple* future milestones (M2, M3, M4) rather than one clean owner, so a DEC-027-style single-milestone reallocation would misrepresent where the rule is actually satisfied — no one milestone can honestly claim sole ownership of "every listed entity soft-deletes correctly". | DEC-051 | `template-versions/:id/test-send` splits into three separate transactions rather than one: (1) reserve — resolve the actor's own email, run the idempotency check, and insert a `template_test_send` row with `status='sending'`, committed; (2) the SMTP send itself, outside any transaction; (3) record the outcome — `UPDATE ... SET status='sent'` on success, or `status='failed'` on delivery failure, each its own committed transaction. A replayed `Idempotency-Key` whose row is still `sending` returns 409 rather than re-sending, until support resolves it. | The irreversible side effect (a real email leaving the process) must never be inside a transaction that can still roll back after it — the review that opened this node found exactly that bug: send-inside-transaction meant a later failure rolled back both the outcome row and the idempotency record, so a client retry re-sent the same email, defeating the reason `Idempotency-Key` exists. Splitting into reserve → send → record makes the send the one step with no compensating rollback, and gives every crash window (before send, during send, after send before record) an observable, non-duplicating `template_test_send.status`. | Wrap the whole operation in one transaction as originally written — rejected, is the bug this decision fixes. Fire-and-forget the send (mark `sent` optimistically before attempting delivery) — rejected: a delivery failure would then be recorded as a false success with no way to distinguish it from a real send. Queue the send through the worker instead of sending inline — the more scalable long-term shape and likely correct for M5-S3's real campaign sends, but rejected here as out of scope: `sendTest` is a small, synchronous, single-recipient action the handoff overlay expects to resolve immediately, and M3-S3 does not own worker/queue infrastructure. |

The other three overlays either have no real consumer yet (`templatePicker`) or are functionally redundant with a screen that already exists (`templateDetail`, `templateFilter`); building modal shells for them now would be UI theatre, not evidence, and DEC-027/044/045/046/049's own standard is that a surface should not be faked into existence to make a checklist item green. | Build all 5 remaining overlays now — rejected: `templatePicker` cannot be given real content without inventing a fake compose/audience context M4 owns, which would need rebuilding once M4 exists for real. Leave `composePreview`/`sendTest` unbuilt too and re-scope M3-S3's own success conditions retroactively to only require API proof — rejected: the gate's condition 3 is explicit and separate from M3-S3's wording, and a user still could not preview or test-send through the actual product, which is the substance BR-TPL-005/007/011 are meant to guarantee. |
| DEC-061 | Sender credentials are referenced by `secret_ref` (an opaque token stored on `sender_config`); PostgreSQL has no plaintext secret column anywhere in `020_sender_config.sql`. | The API response schema (D-55), audit log, and every log line only ever handle the reference, never the value — a full-table dump or log leak structurally cannot expose a credential, since the credential was never stored in the same database as a queryable column. | Store an encrypted-at-rest credential column directly on `sender_config` — rejected: still couples the credential's lifetime and access surface to the same database as the rest of tenant data, and would require key-management infrastructure this slice does not otherwise need. |
| DEC-062 | The local `SecretStore` implementation retains opaque values outside PostgreSQL, behind a small interface (`put`/`resolve`), leaving a replacement seam for a real secret manager (e.g. Vault, cloud KMS) in a later milestone. | Matches ADR-024/DEC-002's existing pattern of keeping infrastructure seams explicit and swappable rather than committing this early to a specific vendor; no current rule requires a production-grade secret manager, only that plaintext never lands in the queryable database. | Hard-code a specific cloud secret-manager SDK now — rejected as premature vendor lock-in with no current deployment target requiring it. |
| DEC-063 | An unsaved candidate secret may be supplied to `test-connection` only for probing the configuration's own stored host/target, and is never persisted; `Idempotency-Key` is required on every `test-connection` call. | Lets an admin verify a not-yet-saved credential before committing to it (a real UX need — "test before you save") without ever writing an unverified secret to storage, and the idempotency requirement (D-64) prevents a retried probe from double-triggering the SMTP handshake or double-writing audit/status side effects. | Require the secret to be saved before it can be tested — rejected as a worse admin workflow (no way to catch a typo before committing) with no compensating benefit, since the candidate secret is never persisted either way. |
| DEC-064 | The probe target (host/port) always comes from the stored sender configuration (D-53), never from the request body, even though the candidate-secret carve-out (DEC-063) allows an unsaved credential value. | Keeps the outbound-connection destination entirely under the tenant's own already-stored, already-tenant-scoped data — only the credential value is allowed to be ad hoc, not the network destination, which is the actual SSRF-relevant surface. | Allow the request to also override host/port for the "test before save" flow — rejected: would let a caller use the probe endpoint as an arbitrary outbound-connection oracle to any host/port, unrelated to whether the credential itself is saved. |
| DEC-065 | Sender configuration and tenant default-sending-policy endpoints use the reserved `/sender-configs*` and `/sending-policy*` public-contract path prefixes. | Assigned under `PARALLEL-EXECUTION-PROTOCOL.md` (DEC-055) so `M5-S1` does not collide with the concurrently-running `M4-S2`/`M6-S2` nodes' own OpenAPI path additions on the same shared `contracts/openapi.yaml`. | Reuse or extend an existing prefix (e.g. nesting under `/settings`) — rejected: the protocol's disjoint-prefix rule exists specifically so each node can add and commit its own paths without needing to coordinate live edits to the same document region. |
| DEC-071 | This node (`M5-S1-sender-config`) is returned to Codex rather than closed after each Claude verification checkpoint; Claude's role under `PARALLEL-EXECUTION-PROTOCOL.md` is independent verification and reporting, never fixing another agent's implementation — no source files under Codex's ownership were modified during any of Claude's review checkpoints, including the backend-verification pass (Checkpoint 18). | Keeps the two-agent division of responsibility (Codex writes product code/migrations/contracts/tests; Claude does all image work, post-run review, and every shared-artifact merge) intact even when Claude finds and fully diagnoses a real defect — reporting a precise, reproduced finding is more valuable to the protocol than a quick unilateral fix that Codex never independently confirms. | Have Claude patch the defect directly once found (e.g. the F-6 tablet CSS bug, or a missing HTTP-level test) — rejected: would collapse the independent-verification boundary the whole protocol exists to preserve, and reintroduce exactly the "self-report" risk (D-69/D-70) that a same-agent fix-and-verify cycle carries. |
| DEC-072 | Repair work performed in response to Claude's findings is bounded strictly to what those findings actually named; no migration renumbering was performed, and migrations 019/021 (owned by the concurrently-running `M4-S2`/`M6-S2` nodes) were never touched. | `PARALLEL-EXECUTION-PROTOCOL.md`'s reserved-migration-number scheme (D-45/DEC-055) only holds if every node respects the boundary even under repair pressure — touching a sibling node's reserved migration file to "clean up" would reintroduce the exact last-writer-wins collision the protocol was written to prevent. | Renumber or consolidate migrations while fixing an unrelated defect, on the theory that it's a convenient moment to tidy up — rejected: no finding required it, and it would risk corrupting a concurrently-running sibling node's own in-progress work. |
| DEC-073 | Each independent-verification checkpoint is itself bounded to observation plus the one Claude-owned artifact (`apps/web/e2e/visual-capture.spec.ts`, per protocol §3.4) — screen-level product defects found during a checkpoint (e.g. the tablet F-6 CSS collision) are left for Codex to fix in a subsequent checkpoint, never patched inline by Claude even when the fix would be small. | Preserves the same verify-vs-fix boundary as DEC-071 at the granularity of a single checkpoint rather than the whole node — makes it possible for a later, different reviewer (human or agent) to trust that every non-capture-spec line of product code was authored and fixed by Codex, not silently touched up by whichever agent happened to be reviewing at the time. | Let Claude apply an obviously-small, obviously-correct CSS fix inline during the same checkpoint that found it — rejected: "obviously small" is exactly the kind of judgment call that erodes the boundary over many checkpoints; the protocol draws the line at authorship, not at fix size. |

Over-budget rows are never lost: they stay `status='queued'` in PostgreSQL with `next_retry_at` set to the next minute boundary, and the next scan pass picks them up. | PostgreSQL — not the queue or Redis — owns the authoritative work list (this node's binding sentence); Redis holding rate counters only, never work, means its unavailability can only ever cost latency, never correctness or lost sends. | Fail open (send without rate-limiting) when Redis is unreachable — rejected: would let a Redis outage silently blow through a sender's real-world rate limit and risk provider suspension. |
| DEC-104 | The SMTP `Message-ID` is deterministic: `<{campaignRecipientId}.{executionId}@{fromEmailDomain}>`, identical across re-submissions of the same attempt rather than randomly generated per send call. | Makes a crash-before-record duplicate provider-side identifiable (by Message-ID) rather than a silent second email, and makes A15's "no duplicate" claim testable directly against Mailpit's own captured messages instead of merely argued from code reading. | Generate a fresh random Message-ID per send attempt (the nodemailer default) — rejected: would make a stale-claim-recovery re-submission indistinguishable from a genuine second email at the provider, defeating the whole point of A15's test. |

| DEC-107 | `suppressRecipient()` (BR-SEND-011) is extracted in `apps/worker/src/campaign-send/suppression.ts` (behaviour-preserving, D-108) and transliterated -- not shared via a new package -- to `apps/api/src/webhooks/suppression.ts`. `packages/architecture-tests/src/suppression-parity.test.ts` (ARCH-SUPPRESSION-PARITY) holds the two files' post-conditions in step mechanically. | `apps/api` and `apps/worker` both declare `rootDir: "src"` with no shared workspace-package dependency between them, so cross-app import is structurally impossible, not merely discouraged; the two callers also hold different client types (`pg.PoolClient` vs. TypeORM `EntityManager`), so even a shared module could not share the function body without an adapter layer built for no other reason. Matches DEC-106's `retry-backoff.ts` precedent. | A new `@eow/*` package to host one shared implementation — rejected as disproportionate: sharing two SQL statements that still could not be one implementation across the client-type boundary is not worth a repository-topology change (ADR-004) plus a dependency review (AGENTS.md SS5). |
| DEC-108 | This node fixes the *count-correctness* half of progress monotonicity (D-105: `sent`/`delivered`/`failed` as rollups, never regressing) but computes no percentage and pushes nothing over a realtime channel. | `BR-SEND-003/004/005` (percent, ETA, realtime) are M6-S1's; this node only became able to observe D-105 because it is the node that makes `delivered`/`bounced` reachable at all (DEC-096), so fixing the regression it exposes is in scope even though the fuller progress feature is not. | Defer the D-105 fix to M6-S1 since it "owns progress" — rejected: the regression is caused by this node's own webhook apply and is user-visible (AGENTS.md SS4) the moment CP4 lands; leaving it broken until M6-S1 would ship a known regression on purpose. |
| DEC-109 | No `contracts/asyncapi.yaml` change. The route logs the event id server-side, which is enough to investigate a genuinely misconfigured provider without a schema compromise. | Store unmatched events in a separate, tenant-less quarantine table -- rejected as unbuilt infrastructure for a case (provider misconfiguration or a stale Message-ID) that is rare, logged, and does not lose any tenant's own data. |
