A summary tier purging `campaign_execution`/`campaign_snapshot` — rejected: needs an ADR superseding M4-S4's snapshot immutability trigger and FK work on `export_job`'s `execution_id`, disproportionate to BR-HIS-006's own scope. |
| DEC-141 | Retention is configured per tenant in a new `retention_policy` table (one row per tenant, absent row = deployment default `HISTORY_EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS=365`), exposed only via `GET/PUT /retention-policy` under `settings:manage`. No new screen. | BR-HIS-006's own statement says "theo chính sách tenant" — a global-only env var would contradict the rule's own text. The approved UI handoff's Settings component has only sender-config/default-policy tabs (`screen-catalog.yaml:472-536`); adding a screen would drift from it. | Columns on `sending_policy` — rejected: wrong semantic home, and that table backs the already-approved `UI-CFG-002` screen. Global env var only — rejected per the rule text itself. |
| DEC-142 | The purge writes one `audit_log` row per tenant per run that actually deleted something (`action='history.purged'`), not one row per execution and not one row per tick regardless of outcome. | A first run over a multi-year backlog could otherwise write thousands of immutable audit rows (one per execution); a per-tick row regardless of outcome would spam the audit trail every 60 seconds even when nothing happened. | Per-execution audit rows (the `progress-reconcile.ts` precedent) — rejected as disproportionate to a purge's own bulk nature. Per-tick unconditional audit row — rejected as spam (A6 tests this directly). |
| DEC-143 | `M6-GATE`'s first success condition is corrected (matching M4-GATE's and M5-GATE's own precedent of correcting stale condition text found at evaluation time rather than silently reinterpreting it) to read: *27 of 28 M6 rules closed; `BR-SEND-004` remains `partially_closed` by design.* | Its `M6`-scoped acceptance (p95 under 5s, reconnect from `last_event_id`) is real and tested (`TC-SEND-004`, `TC-SEND-016`, `realtime-campaign.test.ts`, `campaign-realtime.test.ts`, plus this node's own CP6 3-viewport reconnecting evidence). Its remaining dimension is `TC-SEC-016`, a `Performance` case requiring 10,000 concurrent connections and 1,000 events/s against staging infrastructure this host does not have — co-owned by `BR-SEC-005`, which is `not_started` and belongs to `M7-S2-security-hardening`, whose own success condition already reads "TC-SEC-* (17 cases) executing". Force-closing the row here would misrepresent work M7 has not done. | Same compound-status precedent as `DEC-120` (`BR-SCH-004`/`BR-SCH-007`) and `DEC-029` (`BR-GEN-006`) — force-closing the row was rejected for the same reason those precedents reject it. |
| DEC-144 | `BR-NOT-013` stays `closed`, with its row amended to disclose that `notifications.service.ts`'s `purgeExpired()` currently returns the raw `[rows, count]` tuple length (always `2`) rather than the purged row count — the second of D-125's two disclosed instances. | Both call sites (`notifications.service.ts:29`, `:43`) discard the return value, so no rule's observable acceptance is currently wrong; the retention/purge behaviour `BR-NOT-013` asserts (expired notifications are deleted, audit rows are not) is unaffected and tested. The fix plus its own RED test already exist in `.claude/worktrees/kind-wozniak-055662` under the D-125 follow-up task; re-implementing it in the primary tree would collide with that agent's live work and orphan its test. Recorded as `D-134` and left to its owner. | Re-implementing the fix here — rejected: matches the D-125/D-127/D-131 precedent for out-of-rule-ownership defects, and would create exactly the two-owners-for-one-file collision AGENTS.md §2 warns about. |
| DEC-146 | The notification trigger's `sourceEvent` is renamed `quota.threshold` -> `quota.threshold_reached`, and `NOTIFICATION_TRIGGERS`' quota entry flips `wired: false` -> `true`. | The contract wins: `contracts/asyncapi.yaml:132` and `catalog/realtime-events.json` both declare `quota.threshold_reached`, and `ARCH-ASYNCAPI-CONFORMANCE` matches every emitted `event_type` against a declared channel — so the shorter name in `notification-rules.ts:37` was unemittable by construction. | Renaming the published contract and catalogue to match the stale source string — rejected: it would edit a reviewed baseline to accommodate an unreachable code path. |
| DEC-147 | Tenant sending quota is a locked reservation ledger (`quota_reservation`), not a count derived from `message_attempt` at confirmation time. | `BR-CFG-006`'s acceptance is specifically about concurrency ("Khong oversubscribe khi nhieu campaign song song"). A derived count cannot express it; a ledger written inside the same transaction and the same pessimistic lock as the snapshot freeze can, so a 429 rolls the freeze back and leaves no queued campaign behind. Threshold de-duplication is the `quota_threshold_emission` table, which makes `catalog/realtime-events.json`'s declared `quota_period+threshold` key physical rather than application logic. | Counting message attempts at confirmation time — rejected: see D-141's measured race. |
| DEC-151 | The dead-letter queue raises **no** realtime event and **no** notification. | `project.manifest.yaml` freezes `realtime_events: 20` and `notification_rules: 16` as reviewed baselines, `ARCH-ASYNCAPI-CONFORMANCE` asserts `catalog/realtime-events.json` and `contracts/asyncapi.yaml` agree, and `BR-NOT-014`'s trigger catalogue names no DLQ trigger. "DLQ có alert" is satisfied by the `eow_dead_letter_depth` metric plus a runbook alert entry, which is how `runbook.md` already frames alerting. | Adding a 21st realtime channel — rejected: it breaks a frozen reviewed baseline to publish an event nothing consumes. |
| DEC-152 | A collection route satisfies its cross-tenant obligation with a tenant-scoped `200`/`204` containing no foreign rows; item routes must reject outright. | A list endpoint correctly returns the caller's own (possibly empty) collection — demanding `403`/`404` there would assert the wrong behaviour. | Requiring rejection uniformly — rejected as incorrect for collections. |
| DEC-153 | Production TLS terminates at a managed edge; `WEB_ORIGIN`'s scheme drives the `Secure` cookie flag. | `deploy/nginx/default.conf` serves plain HTTP on `:8080` and `quick-deploy.md`'s production boundary already says a managed edge is expected, so the honest evidence for "TLS policy đạt chuẩn tổ chức" is a written, asserted policy rather than a fabricated handshake test. | Fabricating an in-container TLS listener purely to make a test green — rejected. |
| DEC-154 | Stored XSS is asserted at the context-specific **email render boundary**, not by sanitising stored business data on input. | The dangerous path is merge substitution into an email body a mail client executes; sanitising on input would corrupt a legitimate recipient name containing `<`. | Input-time sanitisation — rejected as lossy and in the wrong place. |
| DEC-155 | `BR-SEC-003` stays `partially_closed` after M7-S2 alone. | Its log-redaction evidence (`traceability-plan.yaml:293`) requires a structured logger that does not exist on `main`; building it is `M7-S3-observability`'s own success condition. Claiming closure here would misrepresent work this node cannot do. | Closing it on the payload half alone — rejected. |
| DEC-161 | `GET /health` keeps its exact current semantics (liveness); readiness becomes a sibling `GET /health/ready`. | `compose.yaml`'s healthcheck and three other nodes' running stacks depend on `/health`'s behaviour; changing it in place mid-batch would have altered the gate `worker`, `scheduler` and `web` all wait on. Liveness and readiness are different questions and now have different endpoints. | Repurposing `/health` into a probing endpoint — rejected as a mid-batch change to a shared gate. |
| DEC-167 | `BR-SEC-004` closes on a rehearsal that **met** its targets here, with the variance report retained rather than discarded. | Measured RTO 10.094s and RPO age 31.203s are inside the 15-minute/4-hour targets *at this data volume*, so the rule's first branch is satisfied. The variance report stays because it documents the real limit — `pg_dump` yields an RPO equal to the backup *interval*, and a 15-minute production RPO needs WAL archiving that `compose.yaml`'s `postgres` service does not configure. Deleting it would convert an honest constraint into an implied guarantee. | Closing on the measurement alone and dropping the report — rejected. |
| DEC-156 | Pino and OpenTelemetry are added as server-only runtime dependencies. | `ADR-017` (Accepted) already decided "Pino structured logs + OpenTelemetry traces/metrics"; this implements it rather than re-deciding it. Licence, security, maintenance and bundle impact reviewed per AGENTS.md §5; nothing enters `apps/web`. | `prom-client` - rejected: lighter, but it covers only the metric half while the trace half needs OTel anyway, so the repository would carry two telemetry stacks. |
| DEC-157 | Metrics are exposed on separate backend-network listeners (api `:9464`, worker `:9465`), not under `/api/v1`. | A scraper holds no session, so serving them on the versioned prefix would force either a `@Public()` endpoint reachable through the nginx edge or a service account. Neither is needed when the listener simply is not published. Consequently no OpenAPI operation was added. | Serving `/api/v1/metrics` behind `SETTINGS_MANAGE` - rejected for the reason above. |
| DEC-158 | Prometheus and Grafana are opt-in behind `--profile observability`; the default one-command deployment is unchanged. | Adding them to the default stack would make `docker compose up -d --build --wait` depend on two more healthy containers that are not part of the product. Both config forms validate, and the profile was started and verified during review. | Adding them to the default stack - rejected. |
| DEC-159 | `protobufjs` install scripts stay denied. | The distributed OpenTelemetry packages work without executing that transitive build script. | Allowing the script - rejected as unnecessary execution at install time. |
| DEC-160 | One mutable AsyncLocalStorage request store is seeded before routing and enriched after authentication. | Starting a second store post-auth would give the pre-auth and post-auth halves of one request different trace ids, breaking the `EXECPLAN` D-26 guarantee that the Problem response's `traceId` matches the `audit_log` row. | A second store - rejected. |
| DEC-168 | The per-message `provider.send` span M7-S3 specified as review follow-up 2 is **not** added; the batch-level `provider.send_batch` span stands. | The batch span already closes the API -> outbox -> job -> provider -> webhook chain `BR-SEND-013` asks for. A per-recipient span emits one span per message - 100,000 per campaign at `BR-SEC-006`'s own stated target - to buy per-recipient latency attribution that `eow_send_attempt_total{result}` already provides in aggregate. Span volume is a cost paid in production, not in the test suite. | Applying the follow-up verbatim - rejected on volume; revisit only with sampling configured. |
| DEC-169 | Forward migration `066_relay_trace_id.sql` uses `DROP FUNCTION` then `CREATE FUNCTION`, not `CREATE OR REPLACE` as M7-S3's follow-up instruction specified. | PostgreSQL refuses `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` when the return type changes - "cannot change return type of existing function" - which is exactly what adding `trace_id` to a `RETURNS TABLE` does. The whole file runs inside `migrate.sh`'s single transaction, so the drop and recreate are atomic and no concurrent relay run can observe the function missing. Published migration `035` is untouched. | Editing `035` - rejected outright (AGENTS.md §5). |
| DEC-166 | `state.json`'s M7-S1 success condition names 80/90/100% threshold emission, which `BR-CFG-006`'s own acceptance text does not mention. The condition stands, and `catalog/realtime-events.json` is recorded as the authority for it. | The thresholds are genuinely catalogued — `catalog/realtime-events.json` defines `quota.threshold_reached` with purpose "Configured 80/90/100 percent sending quota threshold", cadence "Per threshold" and dedupe `quota_period+threshold` — they are simply written down somewhere other than the rule's acceptance summary. Both halves were implemented. Same correct-the-record precedent as DEC-143, M4-GATE and M5-GATE. | Narrowing M7-S1 to `BR-CFG-006`'s literal acceptance text and dropping the threshold event — rejected: it would leave a declared AsyncAPI channel and a pre-registered `owner: 'M7'` notification trigger permanently unimplemented. |

## 21. Risks and STOP Gates

### Open STOP gates

**None.** No irreducible business ambiguity, no destructive action, no required secret or
paid service, and no security decision needing an owner has been found. Every ambiguity
encountered was resolvable against an Accepted ADR or a source of truth and is recorded in §20.

**PLAN APPROVAL GATE cleared** on 2026-08-10/11 (human approval of the Master ExecPlan,
authorizing autonomous implementation starting at `ui_intake_register` and `arch_contracts`).
Both nodes and `traceability_init` are complete. Implementation is in progress; `M1-S1-signin`
is the next node.

### STOP gates likely to arise during implementation

Per `.agents/approval-policy.yaml`. These are *anticipated*, not currently open.

| Trigger | Milestone | Why it stops |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Choosing a commercial email provider and holding its credentials | M5 | Secret / paid external action |
| Deploying to a production host | M7 | Production deploy |
| Admitting real recipient PII into any environment | M7 | Privacy ownership |
| A destructive restore rehearsal touching real data | M7 | Irreversible data action |
| An irreducible business-rule vs approved-UI conflict | any | AGENTS.md §2.1 |
| Repair budget exhausted with no new evidence | any | `.agents/agent-loop.yaml` |

### Delivery risks

| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `arch_contracts` under-delivers and every slice re-litigates persistence | Medium | High | Treat it as a hard gate; the first cross-tenant repository test must pass before any slice starts |
| Visual equivalence drifts as screens are ported piecemeal | Medium | High | Verbatim DOM + unchanged `globals.css` (DEC-009); per-screen visual evidence at three viewports before the screen closes |
| The 134 rule × 159 test traceability matrix is filled in name only | Medium | High | `traceability.csv` is machine-checkable; gates query it; P0 rules cannot be waived |
| Realtime progress diverges from canonical facts | Medium | High | Counters recomputed from `campaign_recipient`; `eow_progress_reconcile_drift` must be 0; recovery tests for crash, reconnect and duplicate |
| Import and bulk jobs partially apply and leave inconsistent data | Medium | Medium | Per-row atomicity (`BR-IMP-007`), idempotent jobs (`BR-CF-007`), error file, partial-failure reporting |
| Migration 002+ breaks the checksum guard | Low | High | Generalise `migrate.sh` first; DEPLOY-004/005 cover it |
| ADR-019 pressure to adopt a visual builder mid-build | Low | Medium | DEC-005 defers it; a change requires a spike and an Accepted ADR |
| Test suites weakened to keep gates green | Low | High | Explicitly forbidden by `.agents/agent-loop.yaml`; gates assert test counts |
